## Real code findings

**Recurring bug — journal stores TEE-wrapped key where replay needs user-ECIES-wrapped** (3 hits):

- `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:529-531` (minor) — same fallback; should be `String::new()`, not `encrypted_ipns_for_tee.clone().unwrap_or_default()`.
- `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:815-820` (minor) — parent IPNS key wrap failure swallowed via `.ok()` → empty key, breaks replay publish.

**`wrap_key` path inconsistency** (major, 2 hits): `windows/write_ops.rs:148,152,886` call `cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key` while the rest of the file uses `cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key`. Possibly a latent compile error if the winfsp path isn't built in CI.

**Other minors:**
- `fuse/mod.rs:102-114` & `fuse/windows/mod.rs:65-72` — journal dir silently falls back to `temp_dir` (defeats crash durability); add a warning log. (suggestions provided)
- `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1394-1404` — case-sensitive `"not found"` match; use `.to_lowercase()`.

28	
29	# Phase 43: Code Review Report
30	
31	**Reviewed:** 2026-06-12T19:13:45Z
32	**Depth:** standard
33	**Files Reviewed:** 14
34	**Status:** issues_found
35	
36	## Summary
37	
38	The journal primitive itself (`WriteQueue` in `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs`) is solid: fsync-before-return, 0600 perms, vault scoping, skip-on-malformed. The fsync-before-ack ordering in the happy path of both fuser and WinFsp callbacks is correct. However, the replay half of the feature is fundamentally broken, and several advertised behaviors are dead code:
39	
40	1. Replay never publishes the parent IPNS record (no key in journal), yet returns `Ok`, removes the journal entry, and unpins the CID the parent IPNS record still points to. The original UAT orphan bug is NOT fixed by replay, and replay actively risks making existing folder metadata unfetchable.
41	2. Replay misinterprets the journaled ECIES-wrapped IPNS keys as raw 32-byte Ed25519 keys, so `UploadFile` replay with a key can never succeed, and `MkdirPublish` replay writes a TEE-wrapped key where a user-wrapped key belongs.
42	3. `record_failure`, parking, and `SyncStatus::WriteParked` have zero production callers — the retry/park/notify pipeline described in the phase goal does not exist end-to-end.
43	4. The WinFsp mirror does not compile (references to nonexistent types) and Windows never calls `replay_for_vault` at all.
44	5. The fuser release error path acks `reply.ok()` after a journal failure — the exact silent-loss the fsync-before-ack invariant exists to prevent.
45	
46	## Critical Issues
47	
48	### CR-01: Replay removes journal entries without any remote commit and unpins the live parent metadata CID
49	
50	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1080-1101` (callers: `lib.rs:926-933`, `lib.rs:964-973`)
51	**Issue:** `fetch_merge_publish_parent` uploads the merged metadata to IPFS but never publishes an IPNS record (the parent IPNS private key is not journaled — acknowledged in the inline comment). It then:
52	
53	1. Returns `Ok(())`, so `replay_for_vault` calls `journal.remove(&entry.id)` — entry deleted with no confirmed remote commit. The journaled child is still absent from the parent folder listing. This is the exact orphan bug (UAT D-03) the phase claims to close; after one replay pass the journal is empty and the data is permanently orphaned.
54	2. Calls `unpin_content(api, &resolve.cid)` on the CID that the parent IPNS record STILL points to (line 1099). If GC collects it, the parent folder's current metadata becomes unfetchable — the whole subtree disappears. This is new data-loss risk introduced by this phase.
55	3. `coordinator.record_publish(parent_ipns_name, seq)` records a publish that never happened.
56	
57	**Fix:** Journal must carry enough material to complete the parent publish (e.g., the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS private key, unwrappable with `private_key` at replay time — same zero-knowledge posture as `FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted`). Until the publish succeeds (`PublishResult::Success`), `fetch_merge_publish_parent` must return `Err` so the caller keeps the entry. Delete the `unpin_content(&resolve.cid)` call entirely — never unpin a CID that an IPNS record still references.
58	
59	### CR-02: UploadFile replay treats the ECIES-wrapped file IPNS key as a raw Ed25519 key — replay can never succeed
60	
61	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1195-1227` (journal write side: `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:819-832`, `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:882-895`)
62	**Issue:** The release path journals `file_ipns_key_hex` as the ECIES-wrapped key (`wrap_key(k, &fs.public_key)` → ~117+ bytes). `replay_upload_entry` hex-decodes it and does:
63	
64	```rust
65	let ipns_key_arr: [u8; 32] = file_ipns_key.as_slice().try_into()
66	    .map_err(|_| "Invalid file IPNS key length for replay".to_string())?;
67	```
68	
69	A wrapped key is never 32 bytes, so this always errors, the whole `replay_upload_entry` returns `Err`, the entry stays Pending, and is retried (and fails identically) on every subsequent mount, forever. Net effect: no `UploadFile` entry that carries a key is ever replayed or drained. Additionally, step 4 (line 1275-1282) re-wraps the already-wrapped bytes (`hex::decode(k)` then `wrap_key(&bytes, public_key)`), producing a double-wrapped `ipns_private_key_encrypted` that no client can ever decrypt — which would silently corrupt the file pointer if step 3 didn't fail first.
70	
71	**Fix:** In `replay_upload_entry`, unwrap the journaled key with the user's private key before use:
72	
73	```rust
74	let raw_key = cipherbox_crypto::ecies::unwrap_key(&hex::decode(k)?, private_key)?;
75	let ipns_key_arr: [u8; 32] = raw_key.as_slice().try_into()...;
76	```
77	
78	And in step 4 store the journaled wrapped hex as-is (it is already user-wrapped) instead of re-wrapping.
79	
80	### CR-03: MkdirPublish journals the TEE-wrapped IPNS key into a field replayed as the user-wrapped key
81	
82	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:525`, `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:153`, replay side `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1135`
83	**Issue:** `child_ipns_key_hex: encrypted_ipns_for_tee.clone().unwrap_or_default()` stores the IPNS private key wrapped with the **TEE public key** (or an empty string when `tee_public_key` is `None`). `replay_mkdir_entry` writes that value into `FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted`, which everywhere else in the codebase is the **user-ECIES-wrapped** key (`build_folder_metadata` at `lib.rs:619`, rmdir at `write_ops.rs:716`). After replay, the folder's IPNS private key in metadata is either TEE-wrapped (the client cannot unwrap it — folder becomes permanently unpublishable from any client) or empty. This breaks the metadata schema contract and quietly bricks write access to the replayed folder.
84	
85	**Fix:** Journal the user-wrapped key (`wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, &fs.public_key)`) in `child_ipns_key_hex` — it is already computed for the parent metadata path. If the TEE-wrapped key is also needed for replaying the child's initial publish, store both as distinct fields with accurate names (`child_ipns_key_user_wrapped_hex`, `child_ipns_key_tee_wrapped_hex`).
86	
87	### CR-04: fuser release acks `reply.ok()` when journal fsync (or any prepare step) fails — silent data loss
88	
89	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:956-964`
90	**Issue:** When the prepare closure errors — including `fs.journal.put(&journal_entry)?` failing (disk full, journal dir deleted, permission error) and encryption failures — the `Err` arm logs, calls `handle.cleanup()` (deleting the plaintext temp file), then falls through to `reply.ok()` at line 964. The OS is told the close succeeded, no journal entry exists, no upload thread was spawned, and the plaintext temp file has been deleted. The write is gone with zero trace — precisely the failure mode the fsync-before-ack invariant (D-04) exists to prevent.
91	
92	**Fix:**
93	
94	```rust
95	Err(e) => {
96	    log::error!("File upload preparation failed for ino {}: {}", ino, e);
97	    handle.cleanup();
98	    reply.error(libc::EIO);
99	    return;
100	}
101	```
102	
103	(With care to roll back the inode mutations made earlier in the closure — `inode.kind` reset and `pending_content` insert happen before `journal.put`, so a late failure currently also leaves local state claiming the write succeeded.)
104	
105	### CR-05: Windows WinFsp upload path references nonexistent types — `winfsp` feature cannot compile
106	
107	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:748-751`
108	**Issue:** The `UploadSpawnParams` struct added in this phase declares:
109	
110	```rust
111	api: std::sync::Arc<cipherbox_api_client::ApiConfig>,
112	rt: std::sync::Arc<tokio::runtime::Runtime>,
113	coordinator: std::sync::Arc<crate::publish_coordinator::PublishCoordinator>,
114	```
115	
116	- `cipherbox_api_client::ApiConfig` does not exist (the client type is `ApiClient`, `crates/api-client/src/client.rs:17`); `fs.api.clone()` is `Arc<ApiClient>`.
117	- `CipherBoxFS.rt` is `tokio::runtime::Handle`, not `Arc<Runtime>`.
118	- There is no `publish_coordinator` module in the fuse crate; `PublishCoordinator` lives at crate root.
119	
120	This code is gated behind `#[cfg(feature = "winfsp")]`, which is not part of `default` features, so the macOS/Linux build being "clean" proves nothing about it. The Windows mirror of the durability fix is unverified, non-compiling code.
121	
122	**Fix:** Match the fuser struct (`Arc<ApiClient>`, `tokio::runtime::Handle`, `Arc<crate::PublishCoordinator>`) and add a CI check that compiles `--features winfsp` (e.g., `cargo check --no-default-features --features winfsp` on a Windows runner or with a stubbed winfsp dep).
123	
124	### CR-06: Windows mount never calls `replay_for_vault` — journal is write-only on Windows
125	
126	**File:** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/mod.rs:66-72, 332-365`
127	**Issue:** The macOS mount path calls `cipherbox_fuse::replay_for_vault(...)` before mounting (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:233-242`). The Windows mount constructs the same journal and passes it into `CipherBoxFS`, but never replays. On Windows, journaled entries accumulate forever and crashed writes are never recovered — the headline durability guarantee of this phase ("replay on mount") does not exist on Windows. (The Windows coordinator is also not seeded with initial sequences, unlike macOS.)
128	
129	**Fix:** Call `replay_for_vault` after the pre-populate block and before constructing `CipherBoxFS`, mirroring `fuse/mod.rs:233-242`.
130	
131	### CR-07: Retry/park/notify pipeline is dead code — `record_failure` and `SyncStatus::WriteParked` have zero production callers
132	
133	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:245-265`, `crates/sdk/src/state.rs:24-29`, `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:97-136`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs:39-51`
134	**Issue:** Grep confirms `record_failure` is called only from unit tests, and `SyncStatus::WriteParked` is constructed only in tests. The sync daemon emits only `Idle`/`Syncing`/`Error`. Consequences:
135	
136	- `retries` never increments; no entry ever transitions to `Failed`; `max_retries` (injected as 5) is inert.
137	- The tray `WriteParked` state and `send_write_parked_notification` are unreachable.
138	- A background upload failure during a live session is only an error log (`read_ops.rs:948-952`); the entry is never retried until the next mount, and the user is never informed. The phase's stated deliverable — "max-retry exhaustion parks the entry as Failed and surfaces `SyncStatus::WriteParked` → tray + OS notification" — is not implemented anywhere.
139	
140	Additionally, `SyncDaemon` constructs `WriteQueue::default()` (`sync.rs:64`) pointing at `temp_dir/cipherbox-journal-default`, a directory that is never created and is unrelated to the real `cb-journal` dir — the daemon cannot even see the real journal.
141	
142	**Fix:** Inject the real journal (dir) into `SyncDaemon`; in `sync_cycle`, load pending/failed counts via `load_all_for_vault` and emit `WriteParked` accordingly; have the background-upload failure paths (`read_ops.rs:948`, windows `write_ops.rs:1002`) call `record_failure` on the persisted entry.
143	
144	### CR-08: Journal entry removed after ciphertext upload but before the parent folder pointer is committed
145	
146	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:941-943` (Windows: `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:996-997`)
147	**Issue:** The release upload thread removes the journal entry once `upload_content` (and a best-effort `publish_file_metadata`) finish. But the parent folder pointer publish — the thing that makes the file visible — happens later via the debounced publisher (`queue_publish` → `flush_publish_queue`, 1.5s+ debounce), is not journaled, and uses live inode state. A crash or quit in that window leaves a pinned, IPNS-published file that no folder metadata references, with an empty journal: unrecoverable orphan. Also note the entry is removed even when `publish_file_metadata` **failed** (only `log::warn!` at `read_ops.rs:933`), so "removal only after confirmed remote commit" is violated on two counts.
148	
149	**Fix:** Remove the entry only after the parent folder publish for this file is confirmed (e.g., signal back through `UploadComplete`/the debounced publisher, removing the entry when the parent publish containing this `file_meta_ipns_name` succeeds), and only when `publish_file_metadata` succeeded.
150	
151	## Warnings
152	
153	### WR-01: Replay order is nondeterministic — `created_at_ms` is never used
154	
155	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:272-285`, `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:171-222`
156	**Issue:** `load_all_for_vault` returns entries in `read_dir` order, which is filesystem-arbitrary (not insertion order). `ordered_for_replay` only partitions Mkdir-before-Upload and "preserves relative order" of an already-arbitrary input. Nested offline mkdirs (`a/` then `a/b/`) can replay child-before-parent; two writes to the same file can replay newest-then-oldest, resurrecting stale content as the final state.
157	**Fix:** Sort each group by `created_at_ms` (already serialized in every op) before replay.
158	
159	### WR-02: `resolve_folder_key` only searches the root's direct children — replay fails for anything nested deeper than one level
160	
161	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1006-1018`
162	**Issue:** Only `root_meta.children` is scanned for the parent folder's wrapped key. Any journaled write whose parent is two or more levels deep returns "folder IPNS not found in root metadata" on every mount, forever (no retry cap per CR-07). The mounted FS supports arbitrary nesting, so this is a common case, not an edge.
163	**Fix:** Recursive/BFS traversal of folder metadata (resolving each subfolder's key as you descend), or journal the parent's wrapped folder key alongside the entry.
164	
165	### WR-03: Journal file permission race and missing parent-directory fsync
166	
167	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:126-150`
168	**Issue:** (a) The file is created with default (umask-derived) permissions and only chmod'd to 0600 after the ciphertext and wrapped keys are fully written and fsynced — a window where other local users may read it on permissive umasks. (b) `sync_all` on the file does not persist the new directory entry; on ext4/APFS a crash immediately after `put()` can lose the file itself, so the "durable before ack" guarantee is weaker than claimed.
169	**Fix:** (a) `OpenOptions::new().mode(0o600)` (via `std::os::unix::fs::OpenOptionsExt`) at create time. (b) Open and `sync_all()` the journal directory after creating the file.
170	
171	### WR-04: mkdir leaves a ghost inode in the local table when a late prepare step fails
172	
173	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:482-534` (Windows: `platform/windows/write_ops.rs:116-162`)
174	**Issue:** The new folder inode is inserted into `fs.inodes` and the parent's children before `build_folder_metadata` and the newly added `fs.journal.put(...)?`. If either fails, the kernel gets EIO but the local table keeps the directory; subsequent readdir shows a folder the OS believes was never created, and the debounced publisher can publish the phantom entry to remote metadata. `journal.put` adds a new failure point after the insertion.
175	**Fix:** On the `Err` path, remove the inode and the parent-children entry (mirroring `handle_create`'s rollback at `write_ops.rs:230-241`), or perform journal.put before inserting the inode.
176	
177	### WR-05: mkdir-conflict retry path never removes the journal entry after the re-armed publish succeeds
178	
179	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:631-641`, `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:686-692`
180	**Issue:** On parent-publish conflict the journal entry is deliberately retained and `FsEvent::MkdirConflict` re-arms the debounced publisher — but the debounced path (`flush_publish_queue` → `spawn_metadata_publish`) has no knowledge of the journal entry and never removes it on success. The entry stays Pending for the rest of the session and is replayed on the next mount; the only cleanup is replay's `already_present` idempotency short-circuit, which never runs on Windows (CR-06) and sits inside the broken replay path (CR-01).
181	**Fix:** Thread the journal entry id through the conflict event so the successful debounced publish removes it, or have replay's idempotency check be the documented and tested cleanup mechanism once CR-01/CR-06 are fixed.
182	
183	### WR-06: Unbounded journal growth and full-ciphertext-in-JSON design
184	
185	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:120-153`, `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:808-844`
186	**Issue:** Each `UploadFile` entry embeds the entire file ciphertext as base64 inside a JSON document: a 2 GB file becomes a ~2.7 GB allocation in `serde_json::to_vec`, then a multi-GB write + F_FULLFSYNC executed on the single FUSE callback thread (macOS) or while holding the global WinFsp mutex (Windows) — blocking the whole filesystem for the duration, and capable of aborting on memory. There is no size cap, no GC of parked `Failed` entries, and entries from other vaults persist forever after account switch (the `cb-journal` dir is shared and only ever filtered, never pruned).
187	**Fix:** Store ciphertext in a sidecar file (`<id>.bin`) streamed to disk with the JSON holding only its path/hash; cap journaled payload size or fall back to a temp-ciphertext-file reference; add GC for parked entries (age/size budget) and an explicit purge on logout/account deletion.
188	
189	### WR-07: `replay_for_vault` runs inline in mount with no network timeouts
190	
191	**File:** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:233-242`, `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:870-977`
192	**Issue:** Replay awaits raw `resolve_ipns`/`fetch_content`/`upload_content` calls per entry (several round trips each) with none of the `NETWORK_TIMEOUT` discipline used elsewhere in the crate (`block_with_timeout`, `lib.rs:54-69`). A hung connection during replay stalls `mount_filesystem` indefinitely; many entries on a slow link delay mount by minutes.
193	**Fix:** Wrap each entry's replay in `tokio::time::timeout(NETWORK_TIMEOUT * k, ...)`, and/or run replay concurrently with (not before) mount.
194	
195	### WR-08: Empty `file_meta_ipns_name` is journaled and replayed into parent metadata
196	
197	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:803-805` (Windows: `write_ops.rs:866-868`), replay `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1271-1294`
198	**Issue:** `file_meta_ipns_name.clone().unwrap_or_default()` journals an empty string when the inode lacks a file IPNS name. Replay then merges a `FilePointer` with `file_meta_ipns_name: ""` into the remote parent metadata — an invalid child that downstream consumers skip with errors (`build_folder_metadata` logs "has no fileMetaIpnsName") and that the `already_present` check matches against any other empty-keyed entry.
199	**Fix:** Refuse to journal (or refuse to replay) `UploadFile` entries with an empty `file_meta_ipns_name`; treat it as a prepare error.
200	
201	### WR-09: `WriteQueue::Default` points at a nonexistent temp directory
202	
203	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:288-292`, `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:64`
204	**Issue:** `Default` resolves to `temp_dir()/cipherbox-journal-default`, which is never created (the constructor documents "directory must already exist"), so any `put` through it fails; and temp dirs are wiped on reboot, contradicting the durability purpose. Its only consumer is the dead `SyncDaemon.write_queue` field (see CR-07).
205	**Fix:** Remove the `Default` impl and the daemon's `write_queue`/`write_queue_mut` until the daemon is wired to the real journal.
206	
207	## Info
208	
209	### IN-01: Off-by-one in retry accounting
210	
211	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:250`
212	**Issue:** Parking happens when `retries >= max_retries` after the increment path, so `max_retries = 5` yields 6 attempts. Harmless but the name lies.
213	**Fix:** Park when `entry.retries + 1 >= max_retries`, or document the semantics.
214	
215	### IN-02: One bad directory entry aborts the whole journal load
216	
217	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:180-182`
218	**Issue:** `dir_entry.map_err(...)?` fails the entire `load_all_for_vault` on a single transient `read_dir` entry error, unlike the skip-with-warn handling of unreadable/malformed files just below.
219	**Fix:** `continue` with a `log::warn!` instead of `?`.
220	
221	### IN-03: Plaintext file and directory names persisted in journal JSON
222	
223	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:33-34, 50-51`
224	**Issue:** `filename` / `name` are stored in cleartext on disk (0600, local-only). Content and keys are protected, but this is a new at-rest disclosure of vault item names not previously persisted anywhere locally. Worth a threat-model note; not a zero-knowledge violation toward the server.
225	**Fix:** Acceptable as-is if documented; alternatively encrypt names with the user public key like other metadata.
226	
227	### IN-04: `sanitize_error` path scrubbing misses Windows and other Unix paths
228	
229	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:226-245`
230	**Issue:** Only `/Users/` and `/home/` prefixes are scrubbed; `C:\Users\...`, `/var/`, `/tmp/`, `/private/` leak through into tray/notification copy.
231	**Fix:** Extend the prefix list and add a drive-letter pattern.
232	
233	### IN-05: Journal removal errors silently swallowed
234	
235	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:943`, `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:628`, `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:929, 968`
236	**Issue:** `let _ = journal.remove(...)` everywhere. A failed removal means the entry replays later; combined with the idempotency gaps above this can double-publish. At minimum log the error.
237	**Fix:** `if let Err(e) = journal.remove(...) { log::warn!(...) }`.
238	
239	### IN-06: `record_publish` called for a publish that never happened
240	
241	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1097`
242	**Issue:** `fetch_merge_publish_parent` seeds the sequence cache via `record_publish(parent_ipns_name, seq)` despite publishing nothing. Currently masked by CR-01; remove when reworking that function so the cache only reflects confirmed publishes.
243	**Fix:** Delete the call; `resolve_sequence` already updated the cache with the resolved value.
244

All evidence confirms CR-01 is fully fixed. The original problematic `unpin_content(&resolve.cid)` call still exists textually, but its semantics have completely changed: it is now inside the `PublishResult::Success` branch (after a confirmed publish), where `resolve.cid` is the OLD pre-merge CID that the new live IPNS record no longer references. This is exactly what the fix prescribes. I have everything needed.

CR-01 VERDICT: FIXED

Evidence:

1. Journal carries user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key
- Schema: `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:42` (`UploadFile.parent_ipns_key_hex: String`) and `:69` (`MkdirPublish.parent_ipns_key_hex: String`), both documented as "User-ECIES-wrapped... via `cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key`... Never raw, never TEE-wrapped."
- Populated user-wrapped (not TEE) at the write sites:
  - `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:523` — `cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&parent_ipns_key, &fs.public_key)`
  - `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:816` — `cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(raw_key, &fs.public_key)`
  - Windows mirror: `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:148` and `:879` — same `wrap_key(..., &fs.public_key)`.
- Replay unwraps with the user private key: `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1259` and `:1325` — `cipherbox_crypto::ecies::unwrap_key(&wrapped_bytes, private_key)`.

2. `fetch_merge_publish_parent` now publishes IPNS and returns Err on failure
- `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1177-1194` builds, marshals, and calls `publish_ipns` with CAS (`expected_sequence_number: Some(seq...)`).
- `:1115-1117` returns Err if the unwrapped key is empty; `:1196-1206` returns Ok only on `PublishResult::Success`; `:1208-1219` returns Err on `PublishResult::Conflict` (entry retained).

3. `unpin_content(&resolve.cid)` on the live parent CID — semantics fixed
- `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1201` — the call now lives strictly inside the `PublishResult::Success` arm, after the new record is confirmed live. At that point `resolve.cid` (captured at `:1123`, pre-merge) is the OLD CID the live IPNS record no longer references. The function doc at `:1098-1100` states the old CID is unpinned "only after a confirmed `PublishResult::Success`." This matches the required behavior (the data-loss risk — unpinning the still-referenced CID before any publish — is gone).

4. `record_publish` only after confirmed publish
- `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1198` — `coordinator.record_publish(parent_ipns_name, new_seq)` is inside the `Success` arm only. The phantom call on the never-published path is gone (IN-06).

Reasoning: The original three failure modes are all resolved. The journal now durably carries the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS private key in both `JournalOp` variants, so replay can unwrap, sign, and CAS-publish a real IPNS record. `fetch_merge_publish_parent` returns Err on missing-key, Conflict, or publish failure, so the caller (`replay_for_vault`, `lib.rs:932-935` / `:991-994`) only does `journal.remove` after a confirmed remote commit. The `unpin_content(&resolve.cid)` call was not removed but was relocated into the post-Success branch where `resolve.cid` is the stale pre-merge CID — functionally equivalent to and safer than the "remove entirely" suggestion, with no data-loss window. Fix lineage confirmed in commit 4bc1a0278 (CR-01 core) and refined in 7633cf795 (replay parking/first-publish convergence).

No remaining work.

Note (out of scope, not a CR-01 regression): the `.unwrap_or_default()` on the wrap at `write_ops.rs:525`, `read_ops.rs:818-820`, and the Windows sites means a wrap failure silently journals an empty `parent_ipns_key_hex`. That is handled safely downstream — replay returns Err on the empty key (`lib.rs:1254-1255` / `:1320-1321`) and the entry is retained/parked rather than falsely removed — so it does not reopen CR-01, but it would surface only as a parked entry rather than an explicit wrap-time error.

I have full evidence for both findings across all paths. Both are fixed in the current code.

Let me verify my understanding of one subtle point on CR-03: the `.unwrap_or_else(|_| encrypted_ipns_for_tee...)` fallback that CodeRabbit re-flagged. This only triggers if `wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, &fs.public_key)` *fails* — i.e., the Result is `Err`. ECIES wrap with the user's own EC public key has no plausible failure mode here (valid key, fixed-size input), so the fallback is effectively dead. The primary path is correct (USER-wrapped). I'll note this as a non-load-bearing residual.

CR-02 VERDICT: FIXED
Evidence:
- Replay unwrap before [u8;32], crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1350-1386 — inside `replay_upload_entry`, the journaled file key is now ECIES-unwrapped *first*, then cast:
  - `1355-1358`: `let file_ipns_key_wrapped = hex::decode(file_ipns_key_hex_str)...; let file_ipns_key_raw = cipherbox_crypto::ecies::unwrap_key(&file_ipns_key_wrapped, private_key)...`
  - `1380-1386`: `let ipns_key_arr: [u8; 32] = file_ipns_key.as_slice().try_into()...` (now operating on the unwrapped 32 raw bytes, with an error message "after unwrap (got {} bytes, expected 32)").
- Double-wrap in step 4 gone, crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1470-1472 — the FilePointer stores the journaled hex AS-IS: `ipns_private_key_encrypted: file_ipns_key_hex.map(|k| k.to_string())`, with the explicit comment "store the journaled file_ipns_key_hex AS-IS... Do NOT re-wrap".
- Journal write sides confirm the field is user-ECIES-wrapped, so unwrap-on-replay is correct: crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:838-851 and crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:908-921 both do `cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(k, &fs.public_key)` (with an inode `file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex` fallback that is also user-wrapped).

Reasoning: The original bug was casting the ~117-byte ECIES-wrapped key directly to `[u8;32]` (always erroring) plus a re-wrap in step 4 producing a double-wrapped key. The current code unwraps with the user private key to recover the raw 32-byte Ed25519 key before the conversion, and step 4 persists the wrapped hex unchanged. Write and replay sides agree on the user-wrapped form. Both halves of the finding are resolved.

CR-03 VERDICT: FIXED
Evidence (write sides + replay side):
- fuser write side, crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:527-531, 539 — `child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped = cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, &fs.public_key)...` is journaled into `child_ipns_key_hex` (line 539). Comment: "journal the user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key so replay writes the correct form into FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted".
- winfsp write side, crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:151-154, 162 — identical: `child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped = cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, &fs.public_key)...` journaled into `child_ipns_key_hex` (line 162).
- replay side, crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1263-1272 — inside `replay_mkdir_entry`, `child_ipns_key_hex` is written as-is into the new folder: `ipns_private_key_encrypted: child_ipns_key_hex.to_string()`, with comment "user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key, matching build_folder_metadata convention" and an explicit no-re-wrap note.
- The TEE-wrapped key (`encrypted_ipns_for_tee`, computed via `wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, tee_key)`) is kept as a separate, accurately-named variable and only used for the live `IpnsPublishRequest.encrypted_ipns_private_key` (write_ops.rs:583, windows/write_ops.rs:201) — it is NOT journaled into the field replayed into `ipns_private_key_encrypted`.

Re the CodeRabbit re-flag (fallbacks to `encrypted_ipns_for_tee` and a `wrap_key` path inconsistency): the only remaining reference to `encrypted_ipns_for_tee` in the child-key path is the `.unwrap_or_else(|_| encrypted_ipns_for_tee.clone().unwrap_or_default())` fallback at write_ops.rs:531 and windows/write_ops.rs:154. This branch executes only if `cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, &fs.public_key)` returns `Err`. ECIES-wrapping a fixed-size key with the user's own valid EC public key has no realistic failure path, so this fallback is effectively dead code; the success path (USER-wrapped) is what is journaled. One cosmetic inconsistency exists but is not load-bearing: the fuser paths call `cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(...)` (the re-export) while winfsp's `encrypted_ipns_for_tee` uses the fully-qualified `cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(...)` — these are the same function, so no behavioral difference.

Reasoning: The original bug journaled the TEE-wrapped key (or empty) into the field that replay writes into `FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted`, which must be user-wrapped, bricking publish. Both write paths now compute and journal the user-wrapped child key, the replay path writes it without re-wrapping, and the TEE-wrapped key is correctly segregated to the live publish request only. CodeRabbit's re-flag is technically pointing at a real `encrypted_ipns_for_tee` fallback reference, but that fallback is unreachable in practice and does not change the journaled value on the normal path; the finding's actual defect (wrong key persisted on replay) is fixed.

This confirms the WinFsp `cleanup` trait method returns `()` (not `Result<(), FspError>`), so the comment's claim is structurally correct — the WinFsp `Cleanup` callback genuinely has no return path to signal an error to the OS. This is a winfsp-rs trait constraint, matching how the real WinFsp `Cleanup` operation is defined (it returns `VOID`).

I have everything I need. Let me finalize the verdict.

**Analysis summary:**

**macOS / fuser path** (`crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs`, `handle_release`):
- Requirement 1 (reply.error + return, no fall-through): **SATISFIED.** Lines 994-1005 — the Err arm logs, calls `handle.cleanup()`, then `reply.error(libc::EIO); return;`. The trailing `reply.ok()` at line 1010 is now only reachable when there was no write to flush (no fall-through from the Err arm).
- Requirement 2 (rollback inode.kind / pending_content): **NOT done, by deliberate decision.** Lines 996-1000 explicitly document that the inode mutations (lines 765-776) and `pending_content.insert` (line 782) are intentionally left in place, arguing the failed-write in-memory state is "consistent with the OS treating the write as failed." No rollback of `inode.kind`, `pending_content`, or the `queue_publish(parent_ino)` (line 794) is performed.

**Windows / winfsp path** (`crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs`, `handle_cleanup`):
- The Err arm (lines 1042-1055) cannot call `reply.error` because the winfsp-rs `cleanup` trait method returns `()` (confirmed in operations.rs:539-548 and write_ops.rs:532-536). This is a real WinFsp constraint (Cleanup returns VOID). It logs and calls `handle.cleanup()`. The mitigation is the same as macOS: `journal.put` is inside the prepare closure, so on Err nothing is persisted, and in-memory inode `cid` stays empty.

The core CR-04 defect — falling through to `reply.ok()` on the prepare-failure path on the platform that *can* signal an error (fuser/macOS) — is fixed. The rollback portion of the "Required fix" is not implemented; it was consciously waived with documented reasoning.

---

CR-04 VERDICT: PARTIALLY_FIXED

Evidence:
- `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:994-1005` (Err arm of `handle_release` prepare match):
  ```rust
  Err(e) => {
      // CR-04: reply EIO and return — do NOT fall through to reply.ok().
      // The inode's kind was already reset and pending_content inserted
      // earlier in the prepare closure; those in-memory mutations remain...
      log::error!("File upload preparation failed for ino {}: {}", ino, e);
      handle.cleanup();
      reply.error(libc::EIO);
      return;
  }
  ```
  The trailing `reply.ok()` is now at line 1010, no longer reachable from this arm. The prepare closure's `fs.journal.put(&journal_entry)?` is at line 867.
- Pre-`journal.put` inode mutations that are NOT rolled back: `inode.kind = InodeKind::File {...}` at `read_ops.rs:765-776`, `fs.pending_content.insert(ino, plaintext)` at line 782, and `fs.queue_publish(parent_ino, true)` at line 794.
- Windows mirror `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:1042-1055`: Err arm logs + `handle.cleanup()` only, no error signal. Comment at 1043-1052 states WinFsp has no `reply.error` equivalent. Confirmed by the trait: `cleanup(...)` returns `()` (operations.rs:539-548; handle_cleanup signature at write_ops.rs:532-536), with the same `fs.journal.put(&journal_entry)?` placed inside the prepare closure (line 935) so nothing persists on Err.

Reasoning: Requirement 1 of the fix is genuinely closed on the fuser/macOS path — the prepare-failure Err arm now returns `reply.error(libc::EIO)` instead of falling through to `reply.ok()`, eliminating the silent "close succeeded" lie. Requirement 2 (rolling back `inode.kind`, `pending_content`, and the queued parent publish) is deliberately NOT implemented; the author documents that the failed in-memory state is intentionally retained. On Windows the WinFsp `Cleanup` callback returns VOID, so an EIO ack is structurally impossible there; the no-persist-on-Err design is the only available mitigation and is in place. Because the dangerous fall-through is fixed but the explicitly-required inode rollback is absent, this is PARTIALLY_FIXED rather than FIXED.

Remaining work (rollback portion of the required fix, if you want CR-04 fully closed):
- In `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs` Err arm (~line 994), before `reply.error(libc::EIO)`, restore the inode to its pre-write state: re-establish `inode.kind` from the captured old values (`old_file_cid`, `old_encrypted_key`, `old_iv`, `old_size`, `old_mode`, `existing_versions` — captured at lines 714-729) and reset `inode.attr.size/blocks`, call `fs.pending_content.remove(&ino)`, and undo `fs.queue_publish(parent_ino, true)` (line 794). Note this requires hoisting those captured-old values out of the prepare closure so they are in scope in the Err arm.
- Apply the same rollback in `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs` Err arm (~line 1042), since the inode mutations there (lines 834-858, 860, 867) are identical; the OS error-signal half cannot be added due to the WinFsp VOID `Cleanup` constraint.

I now have complete verification of the full chain on both paths. The fix is confirmed end-to-end.

The chain is airtight:
- `replay_upload_entry` returns the result of `fetch_merge_publish_parent` as its final expression (lib.rs:1477-1485). That function only returns `Ok(())` after the parent IPNS record is confirmed `Success` (lib.rs:1196-1206) or the child is already present (already_present idempotency, lib.rs:1144-1151). A CAS conflict returns `Err` (lib.rs:1208-1219), retaining the entry.
- `journal.remove(&entry.id)` in the replay loop (lib.rs:994) fires only on that `Ok`.
- The live upload threads on BOTH paths never call remove (read_ops.rs:967-975; windows/write_ops.rs:1006-1012), and `publish_file_metadata` failure is best-effort `log::warn` there but no longer removes the entry — so the "removed even when metadata failed" sub-issue is also moot.

CR-08 VERDICT: FIXED

Evidence:

Fuser path — live upload thread NO LONGER removes the journal entry (`crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:929-991`):
```rust
// CR-08 (mechanism b): do NOT remove the journal entry here.
// The parent folder pointer is published by the debounced
// publisher AFTER this thread exits. ...
// replay's already_present check returns Ok and the caller
// removes the entry once the child is confirmed in the parent
// metadata on the next mount.
let _ = &journal_entry_id; // suppress unused-variable warning
```
The thread does `upload_content` -> sends `UploadComplete` -> best-effort `publish_file_metadata` (failure is only `log::warn!`, read_ops.rs:958) -> returns. No `journal.remove`.

Winfsp path — identical mechanism (`crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:992-1040`):
```rust
// CR-08 mirror, mechanism b: the upload thread never removes
// the journal entry — files without per-file IPNS keys would
// otherwise be removed after upload_content alone, before the
// debounced parent-pointer publish, reopening the orphan
// window. Replay on next mount is the authoritative cleanup
// path (idempotent already_present check), matching the fuser
// path in read_ops.rs.
```
Same: `upload_content` -> `UploadComplete` -> best-effort `publish_file_metadata` (warn-only, line 1024) -> no remove.

Removal is now replay-only and parent-publish-gated (`crates/fuse/src/lib.rs`, single shared `replay_for_vault` used by both platforms via the `#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]` gate):
- `journal.remove(&entry.id)` for UploadFile fires ONLY on `replay_upload_entry(...) == Ok` (lib.rs:991-995). On `Err` it calls `record_failure` and retains the entry (lib.rs:996-1014).
- `replay_upload_entry` ends with `fetch_merge_publish_parent(...).await` as its tail expression (lib.rs:1465-1485) — Step 4 merges this file's `file_meta_ipns_name` pointer into the parent folder.
- `fetch_merge_publish_parent` returns `Ok(())` only after the parent IPNS publish is confirmed `Success` (lib.rs:1196-1206) or the child is already present in the live remote parent metadata (lib.rs:1137-1151). A CAS `Conflict` returns `Err` and retains the entry (lib.rs:1208-1219).

Reasoning: The original orphan window is closed. The live upload thread no longer removes the journal entry at all (on either platform), so a crash after `upload_content`/`publish_file_metadata` but before the debounced parent-pointer publish leaves the entry intact for replay. Removal now happens exclusively in `replay_for_vault`, and only after `replay_upload_entry` -> `fetch_merge_publish_parent` confirms this file's pointer is committed into the parent folder metadata (either freshly published with `PublishResult::Success`, or already present via the idempotent check). The secondary sub-issue (entry removed even when `publish_file_metadata` failed) is also resolved, since that publish is now best-effort with no removal coupled to it.

Remaining work: none.

`cargo check -p cipherbox-fuse` passes (exit 0; the warnings are pre-existing `fuser` vendor noise). **CR-03 residual is fixed** in both the fuser and winfsp paths — the wrap-failure fallback now logs and journals an empty string (→ replay parks the entry) instead of silently writing a TEE-wrapped key into the user-key field. ## Where phase 43 stands now

- **7 of 8 criticals fully fixed** (CR-01, CR-02, CR-05, CR-06, CR-07, CR-08 verified earlier; **CR-03 fixed just now**).
- **CR-04 — one real decision left.** The dangerous `reply.ok()` fall-through is fixed (now `reply.error(EIO)` on the fuser path). But the inode rollback the finding asked for was **deliberately waived** with a code comment. Residual: after a prepare failure, `inode.kind`/`pending_content` stay mutated and `queue_publish(parent)` already fired — so the debounced publisher could emit parent metadata for a write the OS was told failed. Narrow, but real.

## Leftover CodeRabbit items (none are CRs — all minor/cosmetic)

1. Parent-key wrap failures are still silent (`.unwrap_or_default()`) in `read_ops.rs`, `write_ops.rs`, `windows/write_ops.rs` — add a warn, same theme as the CR-03 fix.
2. `wrap_key` path inconsistency in `windows/write_ops.rs` (148/152/886) — cosmetic (re-export of the same fn), CodeRabbit rated it major.
3. Journal dir silently falls back to `temp_dir` → add a warn (`fuse/mod.rs`, `fuse/windows/mod.rs`) — defeats crash durability silently.
4. `lib.rs` case-sensitive `"not found"` match → `.to_lowercase()`.
5. The two "doc" findings just want `43-REVIEW.md`/`43-08-SUMMARY.md` updated with CR resolution status — now accurate to do (7/8 were already fixed, CR-03 now too).

**Recommended path:** I implement the CR-04 rollback (the only substantive one), batch the 5 cleanups above, re-run `cargo check`, reconcile the review docs, and commit it all as one `fix(43)` change. Want me to go ahead — or skip the CR-04 rollback and just take the cleanups + docs?

650	    pub fn handle_release(
651	        fs: &mut CipherBoxFS,
652	        ino: u64,
653	        fh: u64,
654	        reply: ReplyEmpty,
655	    ) {
656	        fs.drain_upload_completions();
657	
658	        let handle = fs.open_files.remove(&fh);
659	
660	        if let Some(handle) = handle {
661	            let is_new_file = handle.temp_path.is_some() && {
662	                fs.inodes.get(ino).map(|i| match &i.kind {
663	                    InodeKind::File { cid, .. } => cid.is_empty(),
664	                    _ => false,
665	                }).unwrap_or(false)
666	            };
667	            let needs_upload = handle.temp_path.is_some() && (handle.dirty || is_new_file);
668	            if needs_upload {
669	                log::debug!("release: uploading ino {} (dirty={}, new={})", ino, handle.dirty, is_new_file);
670	
671	                // Capture spawn parameters from the prepare closure, without spawning yet.
672	                // Journal fsync and handle.cleanup() run before reply.ok() (D-04, D-05).
673	                struct UploadSpawnParams {
674	                    api: std::sync::Arc<cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient>,
675	                    rt: tokio::runtime::Handle,
676	                    upload_tx: std::sync::mpsc::Sender<crate::FsEvent>,
677	                    coordinator: std::sync::Arc<crate::PublishCoordinator>,
678	                    tee_public_key: Option<Vec<u8>>,
679	                    tee_key_epoch: Option<u32>,
680	                    ciphertext: Vec<u8>,
681	                    file_meta: cipherbox_core::folder::FileMetadata,
682	                    file_ipns_private_key: Option<zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>>,
683	                    file_meta_ipns_name: Option<String>,
684	                    folder_key_for_file_meta: Option<Vec<u8>>,
685	                    ino: u64,
686	                    parent_ino: u64,
687	                    old_file_cid: Option<String>,
688	                    pruned_cids: Vec<String>,
689	                    write_gen: u64,

691	                    journal_entry_id: String,
692	                    // CR-07: snapshot carried into spawn closure for record_failure on failure.
693	                    journal_entry_snapshot: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry,
694	                }
695	
696	                let prepare_result = (|| -> Result<UploadSpawnParams, String> {
697	                    let plaintext = handle.read_all()?;
698	
699	                    let mut file_key = cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_file_key();
700	                    let iv = cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_iv();
701	
702	                    let ciphertext = cipherbox_crypto::aes::encrypt_aes_gcm(
703	                        &plaintext, &file_key, &iv,
704	                    )
705	                    .map_err(|e| format!("File encryption failed: {}", e))?;
706	
707	                    let wrapped_key = cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(

711	
712	                    cipherbox_crypto::utils::clear_bytes(&mut file_key);

717	                            match &inode.kind {
718	                                InodeKind::File {

720	                                    versions, file_ipns_private_key, file_meta_ipns_name, ..
721	                                } => (

728	                            }

741	                        .as_millis() as u64;

743	                    let (new_versions, pruned_cids) = crate::helpers::apply_versioning(

748	                        old_size,

```
## Post-Review Resolution (2026-06-14)

All 8 critical findings (CR-01..CR-08) were verified resolved via a code cross-check against the current implementation and a CodeRabbit re-review on 2026-06-14.

| Finding | Status | Resolution (commits) |
| ------- | ------ | -------------------- |
| CR-01 | FIXED | Replay now journals the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key, CAS-publishes the parent IPNS record, returns `Err` until `PublishResult::Success` (entry retained on failure), and unpins only in the post-Success arm against the stale pre-merge CID (4bc1a0278, 0b8545bad, 7633cf795). |
| CR-02 | FIXED | `replay_upload_entry` now unwraps the journaled key with the user private key before the `[u8;32]` conversion, and step 4 stores the journaled wrapped hex as-is with no double-wrap (4bc1a0278, 0b8545bad). |
| CR-03 | FIXED | Both fuser and winfsp paths journal the user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key; the TEE-wrapped key is segregated to the live publish request, the residual TEE fallback was removed, and journal IPNS-key wraps now warn-and-empty on failure (this change). |
| CR-04 | FIXED | The fuser prepare-failure path returns `reply.error(EIO)` instead of `reply.ok()` and rolls back inode/pending_content/queued-publish state; Windows Cleanup returns VOID, so its no-persist-on-error design is the mitigation (4e8c48020, this change). |
| CR-05 | FIXED | `UploadSpawnParams` now uses `Arc<ApiClient>`, `tokio::runtime::Handle`, and `Arc<crate::PublishCoordinator>`; CI compiles `cargo check --features winfsp` (963468eed). |
| CR-06 | FIXED | Windows mount now calls `replay_for_vault` and seeds the coordinator, mirroring macOS (ad2339d7e). |
| CR-07 | FIXED | `SyncDaemon` is wired to the real cb-journal `WriteQueue` (Default impl removed); `sync_cycle` loads entries via `load_all_for_vault` and emits `SyncStatus::WriteParked`, background-upload failures call `record_failure`, and the tray surfaces WriteParked plus a notification (d5cae52fc, 0d4cc08c4, 7633cf795, 7a9f3ca75, 5b6455c34). |
| CR-08 | FIXED | Entry removal is now replay-only and parent-publish-gated; the live upload thread no longer removes entries and `publish_file_metadata` failure no longer triggers removal (4e8c48020, 293de3f4c). |
```

### File 2: `.planning/phases/43-fuse-write-durability/43-08-SUMMARY.md`

> AGENT

Now I'll do the CR-04 fix properly. The cleanest approach: the only failure point after the mutations is `journal.put` (line 874). Rather than snapshot-and-rollback (which would need to undo `queue_publish` — no clean API), I'll **defer all three in-memory mutations until after the journal fsync commits**. That makes journal.put the commit point — a prepare failure mutates nothing, so the Err arm needs no rollback and the debounced publisher can never emit metadata for a failed write. This matches the phase's own "durable before mutate" philosophy.

Applying to the fuser path first (compile-checkable):

  "new_string": "                        // CR-04 mirror: WinFsp handle_cleanup returns () and cannot return a\n                        // status code, so there is no equivalent to reply.error(libc::EIO).\n                        // All in-memory mutations are deferred until after the journal fsync\n                        // (inside the prepare closure), so on Err nothing was committed: no\n                        // journal entry on disk, and the inode/pending_content/publish-queue\n                        // state is unchanged from before the write. A subsequent read returns\n                        // the original data — the failed write simply did not take effect."
}
```

253	| CR-03 | FIXED | Both fuser and winfsp paths journal the user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key; the TEE-wrapped key is segregated to the live publish request, the residual TEE fallback was removed, and journal IPNS-key wraps now warn-and-empty on failure (this change). |
254	| CR-04 | FIXED | The fuser prepare-failure path returns `reply.error(EIO)` instead of `reply.ok()` and rolls back inode/pending_content/queued-publish state; Windows Cleanup returns VOID, so its no-persist-on-error design is the mitigation (4e8c48020, this change). |
255	| CR-05 | FIXED | `UploadSpawnParams` now uses `Arc<ApiClient>`, `tokio::runtime::Handle`, and `Arc<crate::PublishCoordinator>`; CI compiles `cargo check --features winfsp` (963468eed). |
256	| CR-06 | FIXED | Windows mount now calls `replay_for_vault` and seeds the coordinator, mirroring macOS (ad2339d7e). |
257	| CR-07 | FIXED | `SyncDaemon` is wired to the real cb-journal `WriteQueue` (Default impl removed); `sync_cycle` loads entries via `load_all_for_vault` and emits `SyncStatus::WriteParked`, background-upload failures call `record_failure`, and the tray surfaces WriteParked plus a notification (d5cae52fc, 0d4cc08c4, 7633cf795, 7a9f3ca75, 5b6455c34). |
258	| CR-08 | FIXED | Entry removal is now replay-only and parent-publish-gated; the live upload thread no longer removes entries and `publish_file_metadata` failure no longer triggers removal (4e8c48020, 293de3f4c). |

  "content": "fix(43): close CR-03/CR-04 residuals and apply post-review cleanups\n\nCross-checked 43-REVIEW.md CR-01..CR-08 against current code; six were already\nfixed. This closes the two remaining gaps plus minor CodeRabbit findings.\n\n- CR-03: remove the TEE-wrapped fallback in the child IPNS key journal wrap on\n  both the fuser and winfsp paths. On wrap failure journal an empty string and\n  warn, so replay parks the entry instead of bricking the folder with a key the\n  user cannot unwrap. Apply the same warn-on-failure hardening to every sibling\n  IPNS-key journal wrap (parent x4, file x2).\n- CR-04: defer all in-memory mutations (inode kind/attr, pending_content, queued\n  publish) until after the journal fsync in the release/cleanup write path, so a\n  prepare or journal failure leaves no partial state and the debounced publisher\n  can never emit metadata for a write the OS was told failed. fuser now replies\n  EIO; winfsp Cleanup returns void, so no-mutation-on-failure is its mitigation.\n- Cleanups: normalize wrap_key to ecies::wrap_key in winfsp, log a warning when\n  the journal dir falls back to temp_dir, and lowercase the IPNS not-found match.\n- Docs: record CR-01..CR-08 resolution status in 43-REVIEW.md and reconcile the\n  Self-Check in 43-08-SUMMARY.md.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>\n"

Done. Committed and signed as **`341252d74 fix(43): close CR-03/CR-04 residuals and apply post-review cleanups`** on `feat/fuse-write-durability`, working tree clean.

## What landed (8 files, +170/−84)

- **CR-04** — deferred all in-memory mutations (inode kind/attr, `pending_content`, queued publish) until *after* the journal fsync on both fuser and winfsp release/cleanup paths. A prepare/journal failure now mutates nothing, so the EIO reply needs no rollback and the debounced publisher can't emit metadata for a failed write.
- **CR-03 (full)** — TEE fallback removed; all 8 journal IPNS-key wrap sites now warn-and-park instead of silently degrading.
- **3 cleanups** — `wrap_key`→`ecies::wrap_key` in winfsp; journal-dir `temp_dir` fallback now warns; IPNS not-found match lowercased.
- **Docs** — CR-01–CR-08 resolution table in `43-REVIEW.md`; Self-Check reconciled in `43-08-SUMMARY.md`.

## Verified

- `cargo check` green for `cipherbox-fuse` and `cipherbox-desktop`; winfsp path reviewed by inspection (Windows-only, covered by CI).
- 40 `cipherbox-fuse` tests pass, including `replay_records_failure_and_parks_at_max_retries`.
- markdownlint passed; commit SSH-signed.

Net result: all 8 of the phase-43 review's criticals are now resolved in code, not just on the happy path. CI's winfsp gate will be the final check on the Windows edits.

# SIMPLIFICATION REVIEW — phase-43 (fuse-write-durability)

Reviewed the full diff `158addc..HEAD` over `crates/fuse`, `crates/sdk`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri`. Findings ordered by impact. All are read-quality only — none alter behavior.

## 1. Near-identical `UploadSpawnParams` struct + spawn block duplicated across fuser and winfsp paths
**Files:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:673-925` and `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:770-1000`
**Summary:** The `struct UploadSpawnParams { ... }` definition (~20 fields), the prepare-closure that builds it, and the spawn closure that destructures + uploads are copy-paste with only two real deltas: read_ops carries an extra `ino` field and ends the OK arm with `reply.ok()` / `reply.error(libc::EIO)`, while the winfsp path has no reply (Cleanup can't signal). The `file_ipns_key_hex` journal-building block (read_ops:823-839, windows:897-913), the parent-IPNS-key wrapping block, and the `UploadFile` `JournalEntry` literal are byte-for-byte identical between the two.
**Cost:** ~150 duplicated lines that must be edited in lockstep. The phase already accumulated `CR-04/CR-05/CR-07` fix annotations that had to be applied twice; the next change to journal-entry shape risks the two paths drifting.
**Fix:** Extract a shared helper in `lib.rs` (or a `write_common` module), e.g. `fn build_upload_journal_entry(fs, ino, ciphertext, ...) -> Result<(JournalEntry, UploadSpawnParams), String>` and `fn spawn_upload(params: UploadSpawnParams)`. Keep the platform-specific reply/cleanup ordering in the two callers; everything between "build journal entry" and "spawn the upload thread" is identical and can collapse into one function. This is the single biggest readability win in the diff. (Borderline only in that it spans a feature-gated platform boundary — but the bodies are genuinely identical, so a shared `#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]` helper is safe.)

## 2. `file_ipns_key_hex` nested `.map().map().or_else().flatten()` chain
**Files:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:823-839`, `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:897-913`
**Summary:** The journal field is built as `file_meta_ipns_name.as_ref().map(|_| { file_ipns_private_key.as_ref().map(|k| wrap...).or_else(|| fs.inodes.get(ino)...) }).flatten()` — a `map` whose closure returns `Option`, then `.flatten()`, with a nested `map(...).unwrap_or_else(...)` inside.
**Cost:** Four levels of combinator nesting; the outer `.map(|_| ...).flatten()` is the classic `and_then` anti-pattern, and the discarded `|_|` binding obscures that the outer Option is just a presence gate.
**Fix:** Replace `.map(|_| {...}).flatten()` with `.and_then(|_| {...})`. Better: lift the inner logic into a small named local, e.g.
```rust
let file_ipns_key_hex = file_meta_ipns_name.as_ref().and_then(|_| {
    wrapped_file_ipns_key.or_else(|| cached_file_ipns_key_hex(fs, ino))
});
```
where `wrapped_file_ipns_key` and `cached_file_ipns_key_hex` are computed above as plain `Option<String>`. Same behavior, one level of nesting. (Folds naturally into the helper in finding #1.)

## 3. Two replay match arms in `replay_for_vault` duplicate the `record_failure` logging ladder
**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:929-1015` (MkdirPublish arm ~929-955, UploadFile arm ~988-1015)
**Summary:** Both `JournalOp` arms end with an identical `match journal.record_failure(entry, &e) { Ok(Failed) => error!, Ok(_) => warn!("retry x/y"), Err(re) => warn! }` ladder; the only difference is the `('{}')` filename interpolation in the UploadFile messages.
**Cost:** The three-arm logging ladder is written twice; a change to park/retry logging must be mirrored.
**Fix:** Extract `fn log_replay_failure(journal, entry, label: &str, err: &str)` taking a pre-formatted `label` (e.g. `"MkdirPublish abc"` or `"UploadFile abc ('f.txt')"`) and call it from both arms. The success arms (`Ok(()) => { log; journal.remove }`) are likewise identical and can share the same treatment.

## 4. `ordered_for_replay` — `sort_by_key` closure repeated, and `created_at_ms` extraction duplicated
**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:317-323`
**Summary:** The same `match &e.op { MkdirPublish { created_at_ms } => *created_at_ms, UploadFile { created_at_ms } => *created_at_ms }` closure is passed to both `mkdir_entries.sort_by_key` and `upload_entries.sort_by_key`. Since both variants carry `created_at_ms`, the match is pure boilerplate.
**Cost:** The extractor exists in two `sort_by_key` calls; a third copy already lives implicitly in the design. Minor but trivially removable.
**Fix:** Add a private helper on `JournalEntry` (or a free fn): `fn created_at_ms(e: &JournalEntry) -> u64 { match &e.op { MkdirPublish { created_at_ms, .. } | UploadFile { created_at_ms, .. } => *created_at_ms } }` — note the or-pattern collapses both arms to one. Then `mkdir_entries.sort_by_key(created_at_ms_of)` twice. Removes the repeated match body.

## 5. `journal_entry_id` carried into the upload spawn solely to be suppressed

The actual failure path uses `journal_entry_snapshot` instead.

**Fix:** Drop `journal_entry_id` from the struct, the population, the destructure, and the `let _ = ...` line. `journal_entry_snapshot` already carries `.id` if ever needed. (Conservative: behavior-neutral, it's currently unused.)

**Files:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:516`, `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:739` (and the mkdir variants at `write_ops.rs:516`, `windows/write_ops.rs:144`)
**Summary:** The 4-line "now in millis" incantation appears for `mkdir_created_at_ms` and `now_ms` across all four write paths.

The `queue.rs` `WriteQueue` core (put/remove/load/record_failure), the `state.rs` `WriteParked` variant, the `sync/mod.rs` edge-trigger watermark, and the desktop journal-dir wiring are all straightforward — no simplification needed there beyond #4.

## REUSE review — phase-43 (feat/fuse-write-durability)

Scope reviewed: the diff `158addcc..HEAD` over `crates/fuse`, `crates/sdk`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri`. The new code is overwhelmingly the durable-journal replay path in `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs` plus journal plumbing in `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs`. The journal/queue rewrite, the crypto wrap/unwrap calls, and the new `resolve_folder_key` BFS are all clean on reuse (they call `cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key`/`unwrap_key`, `merge_folder_children`, `encrypt_metadata_to_json`, `coordinator.resolve_sequence`, `next_file_publish_sequence` rather than re-rolling them). Three genuine duplications remain, all in the new replay functions in `lib.rs`.

---

### Finding 1 (highest impact) — `replay_upload_entry` re-implements the whole body of `publish_file_metadata`

- file:line — `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1046-1157` (the `if let Some(file_ipns_key_hex_str) = file_ipns_key_hex { ... }` block inside `replay_upload_entry`)
- summary: The per-file IPNS publish block re-inlines the entire `publish_file_metadata` helper instead of calling it.
- cost: ~70 lines duplicate, near line-for-line, the existing `publish_file_metadata` (`crates/fuse/src/operations.rs:125-201`): the `FileMetadata` encrypt (`encrypt_file_metadata` → `iv_hex` → base64 `data` → `serde_json::json!({iv,data})` → `upload_content`), the `[u8;32]` key cast, `create_ipns_record` → `marshal_ipns_record` → base64, the `(is_first_publish, tee_public_key, tee_key_epoch)` TEE-enrollment match (verbatim, including the "key_epoch missing" error arm), the `IpnsPublishRequest`, and the `Success`/`Conflict` → `record_publish` handling. Two copies of the TEE-enrollment + IPNS-record-publish logic must now be kept in lockstep (e.g. any change to TTL `86_400_000`, TEE wrap, or seq handling must be made twice).
- fix: Call `crate::operations::publish_file_metadata(api, &file_meta_with_cid, &parent_folder_key, &file_ipns_key /*Zeroizing*/, file_meta_ipns_name, &coordinator, tee_public_key, tee_key_epoch, is_first_publish)` (`crates/fuse/src/operations.rs:125`). The replay already produces every argument it needs, and already wraps the key in `zeroize::Zeroizing` at `lib.rs:1055`.
- caveat (why this is "high" not "trivial"): `publish_file_metadata` takes `is_first_publish` as a parameter and internally does `resolve_sequence` (which returns `IpnsNotFound` for a never-created record — confirmed at `crates/api-client/src/ipns.rs:24-25`), so it does *not* itself treat not-found as first-publish. The replay's not-found probe (`lib.rs:1090-1100`) must therefore stay *outside* the helper to compute `is_first_publish`. So the swap is: keep the probe, then delegate the encrypt+publish+TEE tail to `publish_file_metadata`. That removes ~60 of the 70 lines. Conservative note: the helper recomputes the sequence internally (one extra `resolve_sequence` call vs. the probe), which is harmless but worth knowing.

### Finding 2 — `fetch_merge_publish_parent` re-inlines the parent IPNS-record publish tail shared with the live mkdir path

- file:line — `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:865-916` (the `parent_key_arr` cast through the `Success`/`Conflict` match in `fetch_merge_publish_parent`)
- summary: The "encrypt merged metadata → resolve seq → upload → create+marshal+publish parent IPNS record at seq+1 with CAS → on Success record_publish + unpin old CID" sequence duplicates the live mkdir parent-publish.
- cost: This ~40-line tail is the same sequence already written inline in the live mkdir flow at `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:613-664` (and again in `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:~228-280`): `encrypt_metadata_to_json` → `coordinator.resolve_sequence` → `upload_content` → `[u8;32]` parent-key cast → `new_seq = seq+1` → `create_ipns_record` → `marshal_ipns_record` → base64 → `IpnsPublishRequest{ expected_sequence_number: Some(seq) }` → `publish_ipns` → Success: `record_publish(new_seq)` + `unpin_content(old)` / Conflict: warn-and-retain. The replay version already correctly reuses `encrypt_metadata_to_json` and `merge_folder_children`, so only this publish tail is duplicated — but it is now a *third* copy of the CAS-parent-publish logic.
- fix: Extract a shared helper — e.g. `pub(crate) async fn cas_publish_parent(api, parent_ipns_name, parent_ipns_key_raw: &[u8;32], json_bytes, old_cid: Option<&str>, coordinator) -> Result<PublishResult, String>` in `crates/fuse/src/operations.rs` (next to `publish_file_metadata`) — and have all three sites (`fetch_merge_publish_parent`, `write_ops.rs` mkdir, windows `write_ops.rs` mkdir) call it. Note: the two live sites differ slightly (they also do the *child* folder first-publish before the parent publish, and signal `MkdirConflict` on conflict rather than returning Err), so the extraction is the parent-publish tail only, not the whole thread body. Because the live sites pre-date this diff, this is more "the new replay code should have introduced the shared helper rather than adding copy #3" than a strict regression — flag accordingly.

### Finding 3 (borderline / lower impact) — duplicated "build FilePointer / FolderEntry then fetch-merge-publish parent" shape

- file:line — `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:962-982` (`replay_mkdir_entry` building `FolderChild::Folder(FolderEntry{...})` → `fetch_merge_publish_parent`) and `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1162-1182` (`replay_upload_entry` building `FolderChild::File(FilePointer{...})` → `fetch_merge_publish_parent`)
- summary: Both replay functions hand-construct a `FolderChild` with `id: format!("replay-{}", ...)`, `created_at == modified_at == created_at_ms`, then call `fetch_merge_publish_parent`.
- cost: Low — it is only the two `FolderChild` struct literals plus the shared call. This is mild and arguably fine for readability.
- fix: No existing helper covers exactly this (the live paths build these literals inline too), so I would NOT extract a new abstraction just for two call sites. Mentioning only for completeness; treat as acceptable unless Finding 2's helper extraction naturally absorbs it.

---

Net: the actionable wins are Finding 1 (delegate to `publish_file_metadata` — biggest line reduction, clear maintenance hazard around TEE enrollment) and Finding 2 (extract one `cas_publish_parent` helper used by replay + both live mkdir paths). Everything else in the diff — the `WriteQueue`/journal rewrite, `SyncDaemon` wiring, the edge-trigger notification gating, `resolve_folder_key`, and all crypto/hex/base64 usage — already routes through existing helpers and is clean on the reuse angle.

## EFFICIENCY review — phase-43 branch (FUSE write durability)

I reviewed the full diff across `crates/fuse`, `crates/sdk`, and `apps/desktop/src-tauri`. Findings below, hot-path first. None of these change behavior or the deliberate fsync-before-ack ordering.

---

### HOT-PATH findings

**H1 — `load_all_for_vault` reads + JSON-parses every journal file (incl. whole-file ciphertext) on every 30s sync cycle, just to count statuses**
- file: `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:140` (call) → `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:261-346` (`load_all_for_vault`)
- cost: I/O + CPU + memory, every `SYNC_INTERVAL` (30s) tick, forever, even when nothing is parked. For each `*.json` it does `std::fs::read` then `serde_json::from_slice` into a full `JournalEntry` — and for `UploadFile` entries that deserializes the entire base64 ciphertext `String` (a whole file's worth) into memory only to look at `entry.status` and immediately drop it. With N pending uploads of size S, that's ~N×(4/3·S) bytes allocated and parsed per cycle and discarded. On an idle vault with a few large parked writes this is pure waste on a recurring timer.
- fix: the cycle only needs counts of `Pending`/`InProgress`/`Failed`. Add a lightweight `count_statuses_for_vault()` (or `load_summaries`) that either (a) deserializes into a small struct capturing only `vault_root_ipns` + `status` (serde will still scan the ciphertext bytes but won't allocate the `String` if the field is skipped via a `#[serde(skip)]`/partial struct — actually a dedicated `#[derive(Deserialize)]` summary struct that omits `op`'s heavy fields), or (b) stores status in the filename/a sidecar so counting avoids opening the big files at all. Simplest correct win: a `JournalSummary { vault_root_ipns: String, status: JournalEntryStatus }` partial-deserialize target — serde won't materialize the unlisted `op` ciphertext into an owned `String`.

**H2 — whole-file ciphertext base64-encoded synchronously on the FUSE callback thread in `release`/`Cleanup`**
- file: `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:~793` (`base64::...STANDARD.encode(&ciphertext)`); mirror in `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:~186`
- cost: CPU on the FUSE callback thread (fuser) / the WinFsp callback (under the global WinFsp mutex on Windows), per file close. base64 of an N-byte file is an N-byte read + ~1.33N allocation, done inline before `reply.ok()`. This is unavoidable in part because the journal must be fsynced before ack (correct, do not reorder) — but the *encoding* step is the cost, and on Windows it runs while holding the serializing mutex, so it also blocks other FS ops.
- fix: this one is constrained by the fsync-before-ack requirement, so it's largely inherent. The realistic micro-win: store the ciphertext in the journal as raw bytes rather than base64-in-JSON (e.g. write the ciphertext to a sibling `<id>.bin` with the same fsync barrier, keep JSON for metadata). That removes the 1.33× base64 CPU+alloc on every close on the callback thread and the symmetric base64-decode on replay. Behavior/ordering unchanged (still one fsync barrier before ack). If you'd rather not split files, at minimum this is worth a comment acknowledging the cost; flagging because it's per-close on the hot thread.

**H3 — ciphertext is base64-encoded into the journal entry, then the raw `ciphertext: Vec<u8>` is ALSO carried into the spawn struct — two full copies of the file body live simultaneously**
- file: `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs` — `ciphertext_b64` built at ~793 and `ciphertext` moved into `UploadSpawnParams` at ~915; same in `platform/windows/write_ops.rs:~186` + struct field `ciphertext: Vec<u8>`
- cost: memory, per close. At the moment of `put`, you hold `ciphertext` (N bytes) + `ciphertext_b64` (~1.33N bytes) + the serialized JSON `Vec<u8>` (~1.33N) — ~3.6N peak for one file. The `ciphertext_b64` is consumed by `put`, but `ciphertext` is retained for the background `upload_content`. That's intentional (avoids re-reading the temp file), so the retained raw copy is justified; the transient base64+JSON peak is the H2 issue. No separate fix beyond H2 — noting the peak so the two are understood together.

**H4 — `record_failure` (Pending branch) and `update_status` both re-serialize and fsync the entry on the failure path**
- file: `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:375-395` (`record_failure`), `:351-364` (`update_status` → `put`)
- cost: I/O on the upload-failure path. `update_status` does read → parse → mutate → `put` (which fsyncs file + fsyncs parent dir). `record_failure`'s Failed branch calls `update_status` (an extra read+parse of a file the caller already holds in `entry`), and the Pending branch clones `entry`, bumps retries, and `put`s. This is cold-ish (only on upload failure / per-retry), but the Failed branch's re-read is strictly redundant: the caller passes `entry` by reference already.
- fix: in `record_failure`'s Failed branch, construct the updated entry from the `entry` argument (clone + set `status`) and call `put` directly, instead of `update_status(id, …)` which re-reads the file from disk. Saves one `read`+`from_slice` of a (potentially ciphertext-heavy) file per park. Low severity but trivial and on a heavy file.

---

### COLD-PATH findings (mount/replay only)

**C1 — `resolve_folder_key` re-walks the vault tree from root for every replayed entry**
- file: `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:260-318` (`resolve_folder_key`), called per-entry from `replay_mkdir_entry:472` and `replay_upload_entry:569`
- cost: network I/O at mount, O(entries × tree). Each call BFS-descends from root, doing `resolve_ipns` + `fetch_content` + decrypt per node until it finds the target `parent_folder_ipns_name`. If a crash left 10 files queued under the same folder, the parent folder key is resolved 10 times, each time re-resolving/fetching the whole path from root. Folder keys don't change within a single replay pass.
- fix: memoize resolved folder keys in `replay_for_vault` — a `HashMap<String /*ipns_name*/, Vec<u8> /*folder_key*/>` threaded through the per-entry calls, seeded with `root_ipns_name → root_folder_key`. Replays sharing a parent then resolve it once. Mount-only, but mount latency is user-visible and this is super-linear; worth doing.

**C2 — replayed entries processed strictly sequentially, awaited one-by-one**
- file: `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:129-247` (the `for entry in &ordered` loop, each `replay_*` `.await`ed)
- cost: latency at mount. Each entry does several sequential network round-trips (resolve, fetch, upload, publish); the loop awaits each fully before the next. D-08 requires all `MkdirPublish` before all `UploadFile`, and parent CAS-publishes to the same IPNS name must serialize (the coordinator lock enforces that) — but independent uploads (`upload_content` of ciphertext, and entries targeting *different* parents) have no ordering dependency.
- fix: conservatively, the `upload_content` (IPFS pin) step of each `UploadFile` is order-independent and could be issued concurrently (bounded, e.g. `buffer_unordered`) before the serialized parent-merge step. Be careful: the parent-merge/publish must stay serialized per parent IPNS (the coordinator already does this), and mkdir-before-upload global ordering must hold. Given the ordering guarantees in play, I'd flag this as "possible but needs care" rather than a clean win — only pursue if mount-time replay latency is a real concern. Listing for completeness; do not reorder the publish phase.

**C3 — `ordered_for_replay` allocates two `Vec`s, two sort key closures duplicated**
- file: `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:410-434`
- cost: negligible (mount-only, small N). Noting only because the two `sort_by_key` closures are identical and could share a helper `created_at_ms(&JournalEntry) -> u64`. Pure readability, not a real efficiency issue.

---

### Things that looked suspicious but are FINE
- The many `.clone()`s of `fs.journal` (`WriteQueue` is `Clone`) are cheap — it's just a `PathBuf` + `u32`, not the journal contents. Correct.
- `journal_entry.clone()` for the failure snapshot in `read_ops.rs` clones the base64 ciphertext string (heavy), but it's required for `record_failure` after `put` moves the original — and it's per-close, not per-write. Acceptable given the design; the real cost is the base64 representation (H2), not the clone itself.
- The per-`MkdirPublish` `encrypted_folder_key_hex.clone()` (write_ops + windows) is a small hex string — fine.
- `last_notified_failed` edge-trigger atomic in `sync/mod.rs` — cheap, correct, avoids notification spam. Good.

---

### Priority recommendation
Fix **H1** first (recurring 30s waste, grows with parked-file size — the only finding that bites continuously on an idle system) and **C1** (super-linear mount latency, easy memoization win). **H4** is a trivial redundant re-read. **H2/H3** are inherent to the fsync-before-ack design unless you move ciphertext out of JSON into a raw sibling file — worth it if write-close latency on large files matters, but it's a structural change, not a one-liner.
