## ALTITUDE Review — Phase 43 (feat/fuse-write-durability)

Scope reviewed: the diff `158addcc..HEAD` across `crates/fuse`, `crates/sdk`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri`. Quality/altitude only — no correctness-bug hunting. NEW-file line numbers.

The journal *engine* (`queue.rs`) and the replay *engine* (`lib.rs` helpers) are at the right altitude — `fetch_merge_publish_parent` / `resolve_folder_key` are properly factored, and `replay_mkdir_entry` / `replay_upload_entry` both funnel into the shared `fetch_merge_publish_parent`. The altitude problems are almost all at the **producer (callback)** boundary and the **wiring** boundary, where the same logic was hand-copied across the fuser and winfsp platforms instead of being lifted into one place.

---

### FINDING 1 — Journal-dir + retry-count construction triplicated across 3 production call sites (small, safe consolidation)

- **Where:** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:102-117`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/mod.rs:65-75`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs:43-47`
- **Summary:** The `data_local_dir().unwrap_or(temp_dir).join("cipherbox").join("cb-journal")` + `create_dir_all` + `WriteQueue::new(dir, 5)` block is copy-pasted in all three sites, each independently encoding both the path convention *and* the magic retry count `5`.
- **Cost:** The whole CR-07 correctness property ("daemon and FUSE must share the same on-disk journal") is enforced *by hand* — three string-builders that must stay byte-identical. The code comments openly concede this ("same path the FUSE mount uses (CR-07)", "mirroring ... mod.rs:135-140"). The moment one site changes (e.g. per-vault subdir, XDG override, different retry budget), the daemon silently watches a different directory than FUSE writes and parked-write notifications break with no error. The `5` is duplicated 3× with no name.
- **Fix:** One constructor that encodes the invariant: `WriteQueue::default_for_app()` (or a free fn `cipherbox_journal_dir() -> PathBuf` + a `const DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES: u32 = 5`) living in `queue.rs`, called by all three sites. This is the typed-helper-encodes-the-invariant-once fix the prompt asks for, and it's in-scope-small. (Note: the old `WriteQueue::default()` was deliberately *removed* in 43-05 to force callers to pass a dir — good — but the replacement should have been a single shared dir-resolver, not three open-coded copies.)

---

### FINDING 2 — Empty-string sentinel for "key could not be wrapped / is missing" threaded through ~8 sites (larger refactor, flag-and-defer)

- **Where producers:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:523-543`, `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:790-810` + `:825-832`, `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:148-166` + `:893-908`. **Where consumers:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1254`, `:1320`, `:1351` (`if parent_ipns_key_hex.is_empty() { return Err(...) }`, `if !file_ipns_key_hex_str.is_empty()`).
- **Summary:** "Wrapping failed" and "no key present" are both encoded as `String::new()`, produced via `.unwrap_or_else(|e| { log::warn!(...); String::new() })` at five producer sites and decoded via `.is_empty()` checks at three consumer sites in replay.
- **Cost:** The invariant "this hex string is either a valid user-ECIES-wrapped key or it means *park on replay*" is re-derived at every site. Each producer repeats the identical `wrap_key(...).map(hex::encode).unwrap_or_else(warn + String::new())` incantation (and the long CR-03 "never fall back to TEE-wrapped" comment is pasted 4×). A new call site that forgets the `.is_empty()` guard will feed an empty hex string into `hex::decode` → `ecies::unwrap_key` and get a confusing crypto error instead of a clean "park". `Option<String>` already exists for the file key (`file_ipns_key_hex: Option<String>`) but `parent_ipns_key_hex` uses the sentinel instead — inconsistent encoding of the *same* "maybe-missing key" concept within one struct.
- **Fix:** A typed wrapper that encodes the invariant once: a small helper `fn wrap_ipns_key_for_journal(raw: &[u8], user_pub: &[u8]) -> Option<String>` (None on wrap failure, logs once) and make the journal fields `Option<String>` uniformly; replay matches `None => return Err("park")`. That collapses 5 producer incantations + 3 consumer guards into one helper + one decode path. This touches the `JournalOp` schema and every producer, so it's a **larger refactor — defer**, but it's the correct right-altitude form and worth a TODO.

---

### FINDING 3 — `UploadSpawnParams` struct + the entire prepare/journal/spawn release path duplicated between fuser and winfsp (larger refactor, flag-and-defer)

- **Where:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:673-...` (fuser `release`) and `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:770-...` (winfsp `Cleanup`).
- **Summary:** Both platforms define a near-identical local `struct UploadSpawnParams { ... }`, then run the same sequence: `read_all` → encrypt → build the `UploadFile` `JournalEntry` (the ~50-line "Build journal entry referencing ciphertext only" block, including the nested `file_ipns_key_hex` wrap-or-fallback closure) → `fs.journal.put()` → deferred in-memory mutation → spawn upload thread → on error `record_failure`. The journal-entry construction (`read_ops.rs:811-845` vs `windows/write_ops.rs:885-918`) is essentially line-for-line identical.
- **Cost:** This is the "same concern handled inconsistently across the fuser path and the winfsp path" case from the prompt. The two copies have *already* drifted: winfsp carries `journal_entry: JournalEntry` while fuser carries both `journal_entry_id: String` *and* `journal_entry_snapshot: JournalEntry` (then `let _ = &journal_entry_id;` to suppress unused — dead field that exists only because the struct was copied, not shared). Any future change to journal-entry shape, the D-04 fsync-before-ack ordering, or the CR-08 "don't remove entry in upload thread" rule must be made twice and kept in sync by hand; the platforms will keep diverging.
- **Fix:** Lift the platform-independent core into one method on `CipherBoxFS` in `lib.rs`, e.g. `fn build_upload_journal_entry(&self, ino, ciphertext, file_meta_ipns_name, file_ipns_private_key, encrypted_file_key_hex, iv_hex, file_name, file_size, now_ms) -> Result<JournalEntry, String>` (resolves parent_ino, parent IPNS name, wraps parent key, assembles the `UploadFile`). The platform layers keep only their reply/cleanup ordering (`reply.ok()` vs WinFsp's implicit ack) which genuinely differs. Removes the dead `journal_entry_id` field along the way. **Larger refactor — defer**, but it's the highest-value consolidation here because this is the most security-sensitive path (the D-05 plaintext / D-04 fsync ordering should exist exactly once).

---

### FINDING 4 — mkdir journal-entry construction + `MkdirConflict` signalling duplicated between fuser and winfsp (small-to-medium consolidation)

- **Where:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:520-560` (+ conflict arm `:651-666`) and `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:145-183` (+ conflict arm `:259-273`).
- **Summary:** Both build the `MkdirPublish` `JournalEntry` identically (wrap parent key, wrap child key, assemble op, `put`), and both conflict arms now do the same thing: `upload_tx.send(FsEvent::MkdirConflict { parent_ino })` + keep-entry comment. The two CR-03 "never fall back to TEE-wrapped key" comment blocks are verbatim copies.
- **Cost:** Same drift risk as Finding 3, on the mkdir path. The D-11a/D-11b protocol (signal-on-conflict, remove-on-success) now lives in two hand-synced copies; the journal-entry assembly is a third+fourth copy of the key-wrapping incantation from Finding 2.
- **Fix:** A `fn build_mkdir_journal_entry(&self, ipns_name, encrypted_folder_key_hex, ipns_private_key, parent_ipns_name, parent_ipns_key, name, created_at_ms) -> JournalEntry` on `CipherBoxFS`. The spawn/publish bodies legitimately differ in small ways, but entry construction is identical and should be shared. **In-scope-medium** if done alongside Finding 3 (they share the key-wrapping helper from Finding 2); standalone it's small.

---

### FINDING 5 — `ordered_for_replay` sort key duplicated via per-variant match instead of an accessor (in-scope-small)

- **Where:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:474-481` (the two `sort_by_key(|e| match &e.op { MkdirPublish { created_at_ms, .. } => *created_at_ms, UploadFile { created_at_ms, .. } => *created_at_ms })` closures, identical).
- **Summary:** The created-at extraction is written twice as an identical full match over both variants, purely because both variants happen to carry `created_at_ms`.
- **Cost:** Minor, but every new `JournalOp` variant forces editing two match arms, and the two copies can silently disagree. It's a low-altitude expression of "every op has a creation time."
- **Fix:** `impl JournalOp { fn created_at_ms(&self) -> u64 { match self { MkdirPublish { created_at_ms, .. } | UploadFile { created_at_ms, .. } => *created_at_ms } } }`, then `sort_by_key(|e| e.op.created_at_ms())`. In-scope-small. (Same pattern would also DRY the `child_ipns`/`file_meta_ipns` accessor matched twice inside `fetch_merge_publish_parent` at `lib.rs:362-371`.)

---

### FINDING 6 — `"not found"` string-matching as control flow in first-publish detection (flag-as-larger-scope, as instructed)

- **Where:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:625` — `Err(e) if e.to_lowercase().contains("not found") => ... first publish`.
- **Summary:** Replay decides "per-file IPNS record never existed → this is a seq-0 first publish + TEE enrollment" vs "transient error → retain entry" by substring-matching the error *message* of `coordinator.resolve_sequence`.
- **Cost:** This is the textbook fragile case the prompt calls out: a backend/SDK reword of the not-found error (or a localized/wrapped error) silently flips the branch — either creating a duplicate record at seq 0, or parking forever. The control-flow-critical distinction (NotFound vs transient) is invisible to the type system.
- **Fix:** A typed error/result from the resolve path — e.g. `resolve_sequence` returns `Result<Option<u64>, SomeError>` (None == not found) or a `ResolveError::NotFound` variant — so the first-publish branch keys off the type, not a string. The right-altitude fix, but it changes the `PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence` signature and its other callers, so **larger-scope — defer / track**, exactly as the prompt suggests noting.

---

### Altitude that is already right (no action)

- `FsEvent` enum replacing the bare `UploadComplete` channel (`lib.rs:111-117`) — correct generalization; adding `MkdirConflict` as a sibling variant is exactly the right depth, and the `drain_upload_completions` match dispatches cleanly.
- `fetch_merge_publish_parent` (`lib.rs:1104`) — the genuinely shared replay core; both `replay_mkdir_entry` and `replay_upload_entry` delegate to it rather than re-implementing fetch/merge/CAS-publish. Good.
- `resolve_folder_key` BFS (`lib.rs:1031`) — generalizing from "direct children of root" to a depth-capped tree walk is the right-altitude WR-02 fix, not a special-case.
- `record_failure` / `JournalEntryStatus::Failed{last_error}` (`queue.rs:426`) — the park-vs-retry decision is encoded once in the queue and reused by both the runtime upload path and replay. Correct single home.
- The edge-trigger watermark for park notifications (`sync/mod.rs:33-46`) is a single well-placed `AtomicU32`, not threaded through call sites — appropriate.

---

### Priority for the /simplify pass

1. **Finding 1** (journal-dir/retry helper) — do now, small, removes a real CR-07 footgun.
2. **Finding 5** (`created_at_ms()` accessor) — do now, trivial.
3. **Findings 3 + 4** (lift upload/mkdir journal-entry construction into `CipherBoxFS` methods) — the big win; do together so they share the Finding-2 key helper. Also deletes the dead `journal_entry_id` field. Defer if the pass is time-boxed, but this is where future drift will hurt most.
4. **Finding 2** (typed Option for journal keys) — larger; pairs with 3+4.
5. **Finding 6** (typed NotFound) — larger-scope, track as a TODO; touches `PublishCoordinator` API beyond this diff.

199	    pub fn load_all_for_vault(
200	        &self,
201	        vault_root_ipns: &str,
202	    ) -> Result<Vec<JournalEntry>, String> {
203	        let read_dir = std::fs::read_dir(&self.journal_dir)
204	            .map_err(|e| format!("Journal dir read failed: {}", e))?;
205	
206	        let mut entries = Vec::new();
207	
208	        for dir_entry in read_dir {
209	            let dir_entry =
210	                dir_entry.map_err(|e| format!("Journal dir entry error: {}", e))?;
211	            let path = dir_entry.path();
212	
213	            // Only process *.json files.
214	            if path.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()) != Some("json") {
215	                continue;
216	            }
217	
218	            let bytes = match std::fs::read(&path) {
219	                Ok(b) => b,
220	                Err(e) => {
221	                    log::warn!(
222	                        "Journal: failed to read {:?}: {} — skipping",
223	                        path,
224	                        e
225	                    );
226	                    continue;
227	                }
228	            };
229	
230	            // Skip-with-warn on malformed JSON (T-43-03, V5).
231	            let entry: JournalEntry = match serde_json::from_slice(&bytes) {
232	                Ok(e) => e,
233	                Err(e) => {
234	                    log::warn!(
235	                        "Journal: malformed entry at {:?}: {} — skipping",
236	                        path,
237	                        e
238	                    );
239	                    continue;
240	                }
241	            };
242	
243	            // Vault-scoping filter (D-07).
244	            if entry.vault_root_ipns == vault_root_ipns {
245	                entries.push(entry);
246	            }
247	        }
248	
249	        Ok(entries)
250	    }

252	    /// Overwrite the status of an entry on disk.
253	    pub fn update_status(
254	        &self,
255	        id: &str,
256	        status: JournalEntryStatus,
257	    ) -> Result<(), String> {
258	        let path = self.journal_dir.join(format!("{}.json", id));
259	        let bytes = std::fs::read(&path)
260	            .map_err(|e| format!("Journal update_status read failed: {}", e))?;
261	        let mut entry: JournalEntry = serde_json::from_slice(&bytes)
262	            .map_err(|e| format!("Journal update_status parse failed: {}", e))?;
263	        entry.status = status;
264	        self.put(&entry)
265	    }
266	
267	    /// Record a failed attempt for an entry.
268	    ///
269	    /// - If `entry.retries < self.max_retries`: increment retries, persist as Pending, return
270	    ///   `JournalEntryStatus::Pending`.
271	    /// - If `entry.retries >= self.max_retries`: transition to Failed (D-09 — kept on disk,
272	    ///   never silently dropped), persist, and return `JournalEntryStatus::Failed`.
273	    pub fn record_failure(
274	        &self,
275	        entry: &JournalEntry,
276	        error: &str,
277	    ) -> Result<JournalEntryStatus, String> {
278	        if entry.retries >= self.max_retries {
279	            // Park: transition to Failed, keep on disk (D-09).
280	            let status = JournalEntryStatus::Failed {
281	                last_error: error.to_string(),
282	            };
283	            self.update_status(&entry.id, status.clone())?;
284	            Ok(status)
285	        } else {
286	            // Increment retries, stay Pending.
287	            let mut updated = entry.clone();
288	            updated.retries += 1;
289	            updated.status = JournalEntryStatus::Pending;
290	            self.put(&updated)?;
291	            Ok(JournalEntryStatus::Pending)
292	        }
293	    }
294	
295	    /// Return entries re-ordered for safe replay.
296	    ///
297	    /// All `MkdirPublish` entries come before `UploadFile` entries (D-08):
298	    /// a journaled mkdir for a parent folder must replay before file uploads
299	    /// that target that folder.
300	    ///
301	    /// Within each group, entries are sorted ascending by `created_at_ms` (WR-01)
302	    /// so nested mkdirs and repeated writes replay in original creation order,
303	    /// regardless of filesystem `read_dir` ordering.
304	    pub fn ordered_for_replay(entries: Vec<JournalEntry>) -> Vec<JournalEntry> {
305	        let mut mkdir_entries = Vec::new();
306	        let mut upload_entries = Vec::new();
307	
308	        for entry in entries {
309	            match &entry.op {
310	                JournalOp::MkdirPublish { .. } => mkdir_entries.push(entry),
311	                JournalOp::UploadFile { .. } => upload_entries.push(entry),
312	            }
313	        }
314	
315	        // WR-01: sort each group by created_at_ms ascending (stable sort preserves
316	        // relative order of entries with identical timestamps).
317	        mkdir_entries.sort_by_key(|e| match &e.op {
318	            JournalOp::MkdirPublish { created_at_ms, .. } => *created_at_ms,
319	            JournalOp::UploadFile { created_at_ms, .. } => *created_at_ms,
320	        });
321	        upload_entries.sort_by_key(|e| match &e.op {
322	            JournalOp::MkdirPublish { created_at_ms, .. } => *created_at_ms,
323	            JournalOp::UploadFile { created_at_ms, .. } => *created_at_ms,
324	        });
325	
326	        mkdir_entries.extend(upload_entries);
327	        mkdir_entries
328	    }
329	}

39	        /// (`cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key`). Only the user's private key can unwrap it at
40	        /// replay time. Never raw, never TEE-wrapped. Required for CR-01 (replay must
41	        /// sign and publish the parent IPNS record). Part of the D-04 zero-knowledge family.

100	
101	    /// Execute one full sync cycle: poll + process write queue.
102	    async fn sync_cycle(&mut self) {
103	        // Check if authenticated
104	        if !*self.state.is_authenticated.read().await {
105	            return;
106	        }
107	
108	        // Update status to Syncing
109	        (self.status_callback)(SyncStatus::Syncing);
110	
111	        match self.poll().await {
112	            Ok(()) => {
113	                // Transitioned from offline to online
114	                if self.was_offline {
115	                    log::info!("Connectivity restored, resuming sync");
116	                    self.was_offline = false;
117	                }
118	
119	                // Write queue drain is handled by the FUSE layer (Plan 43-02+).
120	                // The sync daemon observes journal counts and surfaces parked writes
121	                // to the user via the status_callback (CR-07).
122	                log::debug!("Sync cycle complete — checking journal for parked writes");
123	
124	                // CR-07: read on-disk Failed counts from the cb-journal and emit
125	                // WriteParked when entries are permanently parked.
126	                //
127	                // Behavior:
128	                //   failed > 0  → emit WriteParked{pending, failed} (fires tray notification);
129	                //                 return immediately without also emitting Idle, so the parked
130	                //                 state remains the user-visible status for this cycle.
131	                //   failed == 0 → emit Idle to keep the tray clean.
132	                //                 pending-only state (transient retries) is silent — the
133	                //                 bridge in sync/mod.rs ignores WriteParked{failed:0} for
134	                //                 notifications, so emitting Idle avoids spurious status flap.
135	                let root_ipns_opt = self.state.root_ipns_name.read().await.clone();
136	                if let Some(root_ipns_name) = root_ipns_opt {
137	                    match self.write_queue.load_all_for_vault(&root_ipns_name) {
138	                        Ok(entries) => {
139	                            let failed = entries
140	                                .iter()
141	                                .filter(|e| matches!(e.status, JournalEntryStatus::Failed { .. }))
142	                                .count() as u32;
143	                            let pending = entries
144	                                .iter()
145	                                .filter(|e| {
146	                                    matches!(
147	                                        e.status,
148	                                        JournalEntryStatus::Pending | JournalEntryStatus::InProgress
149	                                    )
150	                                })
151	                                .count() as u32;
152	                            if failed > 0 {
153	                                (self.status_callback)(SyncStatus::WriteParked { pending, failed });
154	                                return;
155	                            }
156	                        }
157	                        Err(e) => {
158	                            log::warn!("Journal read failed during sync cycle: {} — proceeding to Idle", e);
159	                        }
160	                    }
161	                }
162	
163	                (self.status_callback)(SyncStatus::Idle);
164	            }
165	            Err(e) => {
166	                log::warn!("Sync poll failed: {}", e);
167	
168	                // Determine if this is a network error (offline) or API error
169	                if is_network_error(&e) {
170	                    if !self.was_offline {
171	                        log::info!("Network appears offline, pausing active sync");
172	                        self.was_offline = true;
173	                    }
174	                    (self.status_callback)(SyncStatus::Error("Offline".to_string()));
```

> AGENT

Confirmed for E: `sync_cycle` only *counts* statuses from `load_all_for_vault` — but the disk read of each file still dominates (the partial-deserialize only saves allocation, not I/O), and the cost only bites when many large entries are parked (a degraded state). The real fix is the deferred sidecar-ciphertext refactor (WR-06). So **I'll defer E too** — the half-measure adds API for limited benefit.

Applying the four clean wins: **A** (`created_at_ms` accessor), **B** (dead field), **C** (`and_then`), and **D** (journal-dir helper — pending a dep check). Reading exact current code:

The fuser release path (crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs) and the winfsp cleanup path (crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs) carry ~150 lines of near-identical code that has already started to drift. [refactor] Extract a shared journal-dir + DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES helper. This is a CR-07 footgun: the sync daemon and the FUSE mount must point at the same directory. Clean fix needs a `dirs` dependency in crates/sdk (or a partial helper that takes the resolved base dir).\n\n4. [refactor] Have replay_upload_entry delegate to operations::publish_file_metadata, and extract a shared cas_publish_parent helper used by replay plus both live mkdir paths. crates/fuse/src/lib.rs replay currently re-inlines ~70 lines of publish_file_metadata and a ~40-line CAS-parent-publish tail (a 3rd copy of logic also in write_ops.rs and platform/windows/write_ops.rs). Note: the is_first_publish probe must stay outside publish_file_metadata since resolve_sequence returns NotFound for new records.\n\n5. [perf] Memoize resolve_folder_key during replay_for_vault. It re-walks the vault tree from root (resolve_ipns + fetch_content + decrypt per node) for every replayed entry; cache resolved folder keys in a HashMap<ipns_name, folder_key> seeded with the root. Mount-time replay latency is super-linear when multiple queued entries share a parent folder.\n\n6. [refactor] Replace string-matching on \"not found\" in replay first-publish detection with a typed error/Option. crates/fuse/src/lib.rs keys the seq-0 first-publish vs transient-error branch off e.to_lowercase().contains(\"not found\") from coordinator.resolve_sequence; a reworded error silently flips the branch. Make resolve_sequence return Result<Option<u64>> (or a NotFound variant) so the branch keys off the type. Larger scope: touches PublishCoordinator and its other callers."
}

  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-14T12:37:25.820Z\ntitle: Replace empty-string journal key sentinel with Option<String>\narea: desktop-fuse\nfiles:\n  - crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs\n  - crates/sdk/src/queue.rs\n---\n\n## Problem\n\n\"Wrap failed / no key present\" for a journaled IPNS key is encoded as an empty string\n(`String::new()`), produced via `.unwrap_or_else(|e| { log::warn!(...); String::new() })`\nat ~5 producer sites (`write_ops.rs`, `read_ops.rs`, `platform/windows/write_ops.rs`)\nand decoded via `.is_empty()` checks at 3 replay sites in `lib.rs`. The invariant \"this\nhex is either a valid user-ECIES-wrapped key or it means park-on-replay\" is re-derived\nat every site, and the long CR-03 \"never fall back to TEE-wrapped\" comment is pasted 4x.\n\nIt is also inconsistent: `JournalOp::*::parent_ipns_key_hex` uses the empty-string\nsentinel while `file_ipns_key_hex` already uses `Option<String>` — within the same\nstruct. A new call site that forgets the `.is_empty()` guard feeds an empty string into\n`hex::decode` -> `ecies::unwrap_key` and gets a confusing crypto error instead of a\nclean park.\n\nSurfaced by the phase-43 `/simplify` altitude + simplification reviewers; deferred from\ncommit a1ec69f1b as a larger (schema-touching) refactor.\n\n## Solution\n\nIntroduce one helper, e.g.\n`fn wrap_ipns_key_for_journal(raw: &[u8], user_pub: &[u8]) -> Option<String>` (returns\n`None` on wrap failure, logs once), and make the journal key fields `Option<String>`\nuniformly in `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs` (`parent_ipns_key_hex` joins `file_ipns_key_hex`\nas `Option`). Replay matches `None => return Err(\"park\")` in one decode path. Collapses\n5 producer incantations + 3 consumer guards into one helper + one decode site.\n\nTouches the `JournalOp` schema (serde) — confirm round-trip of old on-disk entries (a\nmissing field deserializes to `None`, which is the correct \"park\" behavior). Do\nalongside the fuser/winfsp consolidation todo so they share the helper.\n"

The invariant is enforced by\nthree hand-copied path builders that must stay byte-identical; the moment one changes\n(per-vault subdir, XDG override, different retry budget) they diverge with no error.\n\nSurfaced by the phase-43 `/simplify` altitude reviewer; deferred from commit a1ec69f1b\nbecause the clean fix needs a new dependency.\n\n## Solution\n\nAdd one constructor/helper that encodes the invariant once — e.g. a\n`pub fn default_journal_dir() -> PathBuf` and `pub const DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES: u32 = 5`\nin `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs` — and call it from all three sites. Note: `WriteQueue::default()` was intentionally removed in 43-05 to force an\nexplicit dir — the replacement should be this single resolver, not three open-coded copies.\n"

Keep the first-publish probe OUTSIDE the helper:\n   `publish_file_metadata` calls `resolve_sequence` internally, which returns NotFound\n   for a brand-new record, so the caller must still compute `is_first_publish` and pass\n   it in. (Pairs with the typed-NotFound todo.)\n2. Extract `pub(crate) async fn cas_publish_parent(api, parent_ipns_name, parent_key_raw,\n   json_bytes, old_cid, coordinator) -> Result<PublishResult, String>` in\n   `operations.rs` and call it from `fetch_merge_publish_parent` plus both live mkdir\n   paths. Note the live sites also do the child first-publish before the parent and\n   signal `MkdirConflict` rather than returning Err — extract only the shared parent\n   tail. Verify replay tests + winfsp CI still pass.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-14-memoize-resolve-folder-key-during-replay.md",
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-14T12:37:25.820Z\ntitle: Memoize resolve_folder_key during replay\narea: desktop-fuse\nfiles:\n  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs\n---\n\n## Problem\n\n`resolve_folder_key` in `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs` BFS-descends the vault tree from the\nroot (a `resolve_ipns` + `fetch_content` + decrypt per node) and is called once per\nreplayed entry from `replay_mkdir_entry` and `replay_upload_entry`. If a crash leaves N\nfiles queued under the same folder, that folder's key is resolved N times, each time\nre-resolving and re-fetching the whole path from root.\n\nMount-time replay latency is therefore super-linear in the number of queued entries\nsharing a parent, and mount latency is user-visible. Folder keys do not change within a\nsingle replay pass. Surfaced by the phase-43 `/simplify` efficiency reviewer (cold-path,\nmount-only); deferred from commit a1ec69f1b.\n\n## Solution\n\nThread a `HashMap<String /* ipns_name */, Vec<u8> /* folder_key */>` cache through\n`replay_for_vault` and the per-entry replay calls, seeded with\n`root_ipns_name -> root_folder_key`. `resolve_folder_key` checks the cache first and\ninserts each key it resolves as it descends, so entries sharing a parent resolve it\nonce. Mount-only change; verify with a replay test that queues multiple entries under\none nested folder.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-14-replace-not-found-string-match-with-typed-error.md",
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-14T12:37:25.820Z\ntitle: Replace not-found string match with a typed error in replay\narea: desktop-fuse\nfiles:\n  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nReplay's first-publish detection keys the \"seq-0 first publish + TEE enrollment\" vs\n\"transient error, retain entry\" branch off a substring match on the error MESSAGE of\n`coordinator.resolve_sequence`:\n\n```rust\nErr(e) if e.to_lowercase().contains(\"not found\") => { /* first publish */ }\n```\n\nThis is fragile control flow: a backend/SDK reword of the not-found error (or a wrapped\n/ localized error) silently flips the branch — either creating a duplicate record at\nseq 0, or parking the entry forever. The NotFound-vs-transient distinction is invisible\nto the type system. Surfaced by the phase-43 `/simplify` altitude reviewer; deferred\nfrom commit a1ec69f1b as larger scope.\n\n## Solution\n\nMake the resolve path return a typed result so the branch keys off the type, not the\nstring: e.g. `resolve_sequence -> Result<Option<u64>, _>` (None == not found) or add a\n`ResolveError::NotFound` variant. Update `PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence` and its\nother callers accordingly. Larger scope: touches the coordinator API beyond the replay\ndiff, so do it as its own change. Pairs with the publish_file_metadata-delegation todo\n(which also depends on first-publish detection).\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Checked everything CI will actually gate on. Here's the pre-PR status:

## Green — ready
- **Builds + tests:** `cipherbox-fuse`, `cipherbox-sdk` compile and pass (40 + 43 tests); `cipherbox-desktop` compiles. CI runs `cargo check/test --workspace` for **both `--features winfsp` and `--features fuse`** (ci.yml:557–605) — that's the real backstop for the winfsp edits I couldn't compile locally.
- **No fmt/clippy gate:** No workflow runs `cargo fmt`/`clippy` (grep came back empty). The fmt "diffs" are repo-wide (the whole codebase isn't rustfmt-styled) and clippy warnings are all pre-existing codebase style (`div_ceil`, "too many arguments", vendored fuse structs) — none introduced by our changes. **Don't run `cargo fmt`** — it'd reformat the entire repo for nothing.
- **Up to date with `main`** (0 commits behind, clean merge) and **no existing PR**.

## One real decision before PR
**Commit hygiene.** The branch is **105 commits vs main — ~47 of them `.planning/`-only** (PLAN/SUMMARY/CONTEXT/REVIEW docs + the todos). A raw PR would bury the ~58 code commits in planning noise. The project ships `/gsd:pr-branch` for exactly this — "create a clean PR branch by filtering out `.planning/` commits." Recommend running it, then opening the PR from the filtered branch. (If your team keeps `.planning/` in PRs, skip it and PR as-is.)

## Worth knowing (not blockers)
- The winfsp CR-03/CR-04/simplify edits are verified **only by CI**, not locally (macOS can't build winfsp). High confidence — they're line-symmetric to the compile-tested fuser path — but the PR's CI run is their first real compile.
- No new unit test asserts the CR-04 "prepare-failure leaves no partial state" behavior (hard to force a `journal.put` failure in a test). Existing replay/park tests pass. Optional to add.

## Suggested PR title (passes the no-parens-in-subject rule)
`feat(fuse): durable write journal with crash-recovery replay`

Want me to run `/gsd:pr-branch` and open the PR, or `/gsd:ship` (PR + review)?

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/.claude/jobs/76fc2ea9/tmp/pr-body.md",
  "content": "## Summary\n\n**Phase 43: FUSE Write Durability** — Verified ✓ (18/18 automated must-haves; human UAT completed on macOS, Linux, and Windows).\n\nMakes FUSE writes durable. `release()` / WinFsp `cleanup()` now journal each pending upload to a crash-safe, fsync-before-ack on-disk journal (`cb-journal`) **before** acking the OS, so a crash or app quit no longer silently loses a write. On mount, `replay_for_vault` replays journaled entries — re-uploading content and signing/publishing the parent IPNS record from a journaled user-ECIES-wrapped key. mkdir parent-publish conflicts now enqueue a retry instead of orphaning the child folder, and exhausted retries park the entry as `Failed` and surface a tray + OS notification.\n\n## Changes\n\n- **Durable write journal** (`crates/sdk/src/queue.rs`): persistent `WriteQueue` with fsync-before-return, `0o600` atomic perms + parent-dir fsync, vault scoping, skip-on-malformed, and a `record_failure` retry/park lifecycle.\n- **Crash-recovery replay** (`crates/fuse/src/lib.rs`): `replay_for_vault` + `replay_mkdir_entry` / `replay_upload_entry`; CAS parent-IPNS publish from journaled user-wrapped keys; BFS `resolve_folder_key`; ordered replay (mkdir-before-upload, then `created_at_ms`).\n- **fsync-before-ack write paths** (`read_ops.rs`, `write_ops.rs`, winfsp `write_ops.rs`): journal the entry before reply; in-memory mutations deferred until **after** the journal commit, so a prepare/journal failure leaves no partial state.\n- **Park → notify pipeline** (`crates/sdk/src/sync.rs`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync`, `tray`): the sync daemon reads the real `cb-journal`, emits `WriteParked`, bridged (edge-triggered) to tray status + an OS notification.\n- **Windows / winfsp parity**: mount calls `replay_for_vault` with a seeded `PublishCoordinator`; journal removal is replay-only.\n\n## Verification\n\n- [x] **Automated**: 18/18 must-haves verified. `cargo check`/`test` green for `cipherbox-fuse` and `cipherbox-sdk`; CI compiles + tests the workspace for **both** `--features winfsp` and `--features fuse`.\n- [x] **Human UAT** (macOS, Linux, Windows — headless UAT passed on all): SIGKILL replay, park-notification render, mkdir-orphan survival, ciphertext-only journal.\n- [x] **Post-review**: all 8 critical findings (CR-01–CR-08) from `43-REVIEW.md` verified resolved via code cross-check + a CodeRabbit re-review.\n\n## Key Decisions & Notes\n\n- CR-03 residual + journal key-wrap hardening, the CR-04 deferred-mutation fix, and 3 cleanups landed post-review (commits `341252d74`, `a1ec69f1b`).\n- 6 deferred quality follow-ups captured as todos under `.planning/todos/pending/` (commit `46b3e1326`): fuser↔winfsp consolidation, `Option<String>` journal keys, shared journal-dir helper, replay delegation to `publish_file_metadata`, `resolve_folder_key` memoization, typed not-found error.\n- Zero-knowledge preserved: the journal stores only ciphertext, user-ECIES-wrapped keys, IVs, and IPNS names — never plaintext.\n\n## Merge note\n\nThe branch predates the squash-merge of phase 42 (PR #485, already in `main`), so the **commit list** includes 22 redundant phase-42 commits that contribute **zero net diff** — the \"Files changed\" view is phase-43-only. Recommend **squash-merge**.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n"
}

  "content": "---\nname: project-gh-pr-create-bot-push\ndescription: After gh pr create, github-actions[bot] pushes a chore(release) commit to the PR branch — the next local push is rejected until fetch+rebase\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nAfter `gh pr create` on cipher-box, `github-actions[bot]` immediately pushes a `chore(release): set release targets for PR #N` commit (release-please automation) onto the PR branch. Any further local `git push` to that branch is then rejected with \"remote contains work that you do not have locally / fetch first\".\n\n**Why:** release-please reacts to the newly opened PR and amends the branch with release targets, so the remote branch advances by one commit the moment the PR is created.\n\n**How to apply:** When you have more local commits to push right after opening a PR (e.g. a `STATE.md` ship-status update), expect the first push to be rejected. Run `git fetch origin <branch>` then `git rebase origin/<branch>` — the bot commit only touches release config, so it rebases clean — and push again. Never force-push. The rebase replays your commit and re-signs it, so the [[project-1password-ssh-signing-hangs]] signing step applies. Pushes also run the slow Entire pre-push checkpoint sync, so run them as unsandboxed background Bash.\n"
}

1	# CipherBox Project Memory
2	
3	## Markdown Lint Rules (pre-commit hook)
4	
5	The repo enforces `markdownlint` via lint-staged on commit. Common violations:
6	
7	- **MD036**: Don't use `**bold text**` as a heading — use proper `###` headings instead
8	- **MD031/MD032**: Blank lines required around fenced code blocks and lists
9	- Italic footers like `*Last updated: ...*` trigger MD036 — use plain text instead
10	
11	When spawning sub-agents that write `.md` files and commit, include this warning:
12	> Markdownlint enforced on commit. Use headings not bold-as-heading. Blank lines around code blocks and lists.
13	
14	## Commit Hooks
15	
16	- **lint-staged**: Runs markdownlint, prettier on `.md` files; eslint on `.ts/.tsx`
17	- **commitlint**: Conventional commits (`feat:`, `fix:`, etc.) + custom rule rejecting parens in subject. As of 2026-06-10 the `.husky/commit-msg` hook is an Entire CLI wrapper that does NOT run commitlint locally — enforcement is via PR-title CI (`pr-title.yml`). Still follow the format.
18	- Package: `@commitlint/cli` + `@commitlint/config-conventional` (had to install manually 2026-02-11)
19	- [Automated commits bypass pre-commit lint](project-automated-commits-bypass-precommit-lint.md) — GSD/Entire commits skip the husky pre-commit hook, so eslint/prettier errors reach CI; CI's `eslint .` is the backstop, fix is usually a trivial `eslint --fix`
20	
21	## GSD Sub-Agent Tips
22	
23	- [GSD subagents must not run full test suites](feedback-gsd-subagents-no-test-runs.md) — checker agents with Bash will run concurrent vitest suites and starve RAM; constrain prompts to "static analysis only"
24	- Sub-agents do NOT automatically receive `~/.claude/learnings/LEARNINGS.md` or project memory
25	- Include critical constraints (markdownlint rules, commit format) in agent prompts explicitly
26	- Research agents write to `.planning/research/` which is gitignored — commits need explicit `git add`
27	- [gsd-security-auditor writes root SECURITY.md](project-gsd-security-auditor-writes-root-securitymd.md) — in /gsd:secure-phase the auditor appends to repo-root SECURITY.md, not the phase path; git restore root and write the phase doc yourself
28	
29	## Git Commit Signing
30	
31	- [1Password SSH signing hangs](project-1password-ssh-signing-hangs.md) — git commit needs unsandboxed background Bash; hung signers wedge 1Password, restart app to recover; PRs enforce signatures so never use --no-gpg-sign
32	- [gsd commit helper false negative](project-gsd-commit-helper-false-negative.md) — `query commit` returns commit_failed while the commit actually lands; verify with git log, never retry
33	
34	## Infra Context
35	
36	- [Phala credits expected](project-phala-credits-expected.md) — staging TEE may return to Phala Cloud; don't flag the inert PHALA_* GitHub staging env entries as orphaned in audits
37	
38	## gh CLI Auth
39	
40	- [gh requires env -u GITHUB_TOKEN](feedback_gh_auth.md) — always prefix gh commands with `env -u GITHUB_TOKEN`
41	
42	## CodeRabbit CLI
43	
44	- [Use --agent not --plain](feedback-coderabbit-cli-use-agent-flag.md) — `coderabbit review --agent` gives structured findings that are easier to parse than `--plain`; keep `--base-commit <sha>` to scope a single phase's diff
45	
46	## GSD Monorepo Gotchas
47	
48	- [Cross-package dist staleness](project-cross-package-dist-staleness.md) — sdk/web tsc check the built dist, not source; rebuild sdk-core/sdk dist before consumer typecheck after public-API changes
49	- [ROADMAP truncation by gsd-planner](project-roadmap-truncation-gsd-planner.md) — gap-mode planner edits can truncate ROADMAP.md; verify `wc -l` + phase count after any agent touches it, restore from git if broken
50	- [Web vitest runs *.test.ts only](project-web-vitest-include-test-only.md) — apps/web vitest `include` is `src/**/*.test.ts`; `.spec.ts` files (the API/jest convention) are silently skipped and never run in CI
51	
52	## Desktop / FUSE
53	
54	- [Headless desktop FUSE UAT recipe](project-headless-desktop-fuse-uat.md) — run the desktop app headlessly (`--dev-key` + local stack) for agent-driven UAT; includes the macFUSE-vs-FUSE-T linking gotcha that silently breaks the mount
55

1	//! Sync daemon bridge for desktop app.
2	//!
3	//! Creates `cipherbox_sdk::SyncDaemon` with Tauri tray status callback.
4	//! The sync daemon itself lives in the SDK crate -- this module provides
5	//! the bridge that converts SDK `SyncStatus` to Tauri `TrayStatus`.
6	
7	// Re-export SDK types that the desktop app uses directly
8	pub use cipherbox_sdk::sync::SYNC_INTERVAL;
9	pub use cipherbox_sdk::SyncDaemon;
10	
11	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
12	use std::sync::Arc;
13	use std::time::Duration;
14	
15	use tokio::sync::mpsc;
16	
17	use cipherbox_sdk::SyncStatus;
18	
19	/// Start the SDK sync daemon with a Tauri tray status bridge.
20	///
21	/// The `sync_now_rx` channel receives manual sync triggers from the tray menu.
22	/// The `write_queue` must point at the same cb-journal directory the FUSE layer writes
23	/// so the daemon can observe `Failed` entries and surface them via `WriteParked` (CR-07).
24	/// Returns a `SyncDaemon` that should be run in a spawned tokio task.
25	pub fn create_sync_daemon(
26	    state: Arc<cipherbox_sdk::KeyState>,
27	    app_handle: tauri::AppHandle,
28	    poll_interval: Duration,
29	    sync_now_rx: mpsc::Receiver<()>,
30	    write_queue: cipherbox_sdk::WriteQueue,
31	) -> SyncDaemon {
32	    let app = app_handle;
33	    // Edge-trigger the park notification. The SDK daemon emits a level-triggered
34	    // WriteParked status on every poll cycle while any entry stays Failed, but the
35	    // OS toast must fire only when the parked-failure count *increases* (a newly
36	    // parked write) — otherwise it re-notifies every ~30s poll (D-10: must not spam
37	    // the user). The tray status itself stays level-triggered (steady-state indicator);
38	    // only the notification is gated. The watermark resets to 0 when the journal clears
39	    // (Idle / failed == 0) so a future failure notifies again. It is intentionally NOT
40	    // reset on Syncing — the daemon emits Syncing at the start of every cycle, so
41	    // resetting there would re-arm the toast each poll and reintroduce the spam.
42	    let last_notified_failed = Arc::new(AtomicU32::new(0));
43	    let status_callback = Arc::new(move |status: SyncStatus| {
44	        // Bridge SDK sync status to Tauri tray status update.
45	        let tray_status = match status {
46	            SyncStatus::Idle => {
47	                last_notified_failed.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
48	                crate::tray::TrayStatus::Synced
49	            }
50	            SyncStatus::Syncing => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Syncing,
51	            SyncStatus::Error(ref e) if e == "Offline" => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Offline,
52	            SyncStatus::Error(e) => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Error(e),
53	            SyncStatus::WriteParked { failed, .. } if failed > 0 => {
54	                // Notify only when the parked-failure count rises above the last
55	                // value we notified on, so the toast fires once per newly parked
56	                // write rather than on every poll. ZK-safe neutral copy — no file
57	                // names or paths that could leak vault contents into OS logs.
58	                let prev = last_notified_failed.swap(failed, Ordering::Relaxed);
59	                if failed > prev {
60	                    let msg = format!("{} pending upload(s) failed and require attention.", failed);
61	                    if let Err(e) = crate::tray::send_write_parked_notification(&app, &msg) {
62	                        log::warn!("Failed to send write-parked notification: {}", e);
63	                    }
64	                }
65	                crate::tray::TrayStatus::WriteParked
66	            }
67	            SyncStatus::WriteParked { .. } => {
68	                // failed == 0: pending-only state — transient retries are silent (D-10).
69	                last_notified_failed.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
70	                crate::tray::TrayStatus::Syncing
71	            }
72	        };
73	        let _ = crate::tray::update_tray_status(&app, &tray_status);
74	    });
75	
76	    SyncDaemon::new(state, status_callback, poll_interval, sync_now_rx, write_queue)
77	}
78	
```

> AGENT

Pulled the codecov config — this reframes the whole thing. Two findings that matter more than the 3.84%:

## The red is non-blocking, and it's measuring the wrong layer

- **`patch` is `informational: true`** (codecov.yml:69) and the **`desktop` flag is `informational: true`** (line 62). So the ❌ is **advisory — it does not block merge**. The actual required checks are Lint/E2E/CodeRabbit/pr-title.
- **`crates/fuse` and `crates/sdk` are in *no* flag path** (only `apps/**`, `packages/**`, and `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/**` are tracked). So the entire journal/replay/queue layer — **including the 40 fuse + 43 sdk tests that pass** — is **invisible to codecov**. The `sdk` flag at 84.62% is the *TypeScript* `packages/sdk`, not the Rust crate.
- That's why all **75 missing lines are in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/`** — the only Rust path codecov sees is the Tauri glue, which is inherently integration-tested, not unit-tested. The 3.84% is the desktop-glue-only number, not your real test situation.

## The 75 lines, triaged

| File | Lines | Testable? |
|---|---|---|
| `fuse/mod.rs` | 35 | No — mount orchestration (journal dir, `replay_for_vault`, coordinator seeding, `CipherBoxFS` build); needs live FUSE + Tauri |
| `sync/mod.rs` | 16 | **Partly** — the edge-trigger notify decision is pure logic worth extracting |
| `commands/sync.rs` | 12 | No — Tauri command wiring |
| `tray/mod.rs` | 9 | No — OS notification / tray render |
| `commands/auth.rs` | 3 | No — Tauri command |

(`tray/status.rs` is already 100% — proof that the *pure* status mapping is testable when separated from the OS calls.)

## Worth doing (both as follow-ups, not merge blockers)

1. **Highest ROI — wire `crates/fuse` + `crates/sdk` into codecov** (a `cargo llvm-cov` job + a `rust` flag/path). This makes your existing 40+43 Rust tests *count* and gives real signal on the durability logic, instead of grading the PR purely on untestable Tauri glue. Infra change, ~1 small CI job.
2. **Small code win — extract + unit-test the `sync/mod.rs` edge-trigger logic.** The "notify only when `failed > prev`, reset on Idle" decision (commit `5b6455c34`, a dedicated anti-spam fix) is subtle and off-by-one-prone but pure — pull it into a `should_notify(prev, failed)` + status→tray mapping (like `tray/status.rs`) and test it.

Everything else is genuinely Tauri/FUSE-mount/OS glue you already validated via the 3-platform headless UAT — not worth chasing, and it's non-blocking anyway.

1	//! Status state machine for the system tray icon.
2	//!
3	//! Represents all possible states of the desktop app as displayed
4	//! in the menu bar tray icon's status line.
5	
6	/// All possible states the desktop app can be in.
7	///
8	/// The tray menu status line displays the human-readable label
9	/// returned by `TrayStatus::label()`.
10	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
11	pub enum TrayStatus {
12	    /// No auth, no mount -- initial state and post-logout state.
13	    NotConnected,
14	    /// Auth complete, FUSE filesystem is mounting.
15	    Mounting,
16	    /// Background sync is actively polling/refreshing metadata.
17	    Syncing,
18	    /// Up to date -- last sync completed successfully.
19	    Synced,
20	    /// Network unavailable -- waiting for connectivity to resume.
21	    Offline,
22	    /// Something went wrong (with human-readable description).
23	    Error(String),
24	    /// One or more uploads permanently failed and are parked on disk (D-10).
25	    ///
26	    /// Displayed when `SyncStatus::WriteParked { failed > 0 }` is received.
27	    WriteParked,
28	}
29	
30	impl TrayStatus {
31	    /// Human-readable status text for the tray menu item.
32	    pub fn label(&self) -> &str {
33	        match self {
34	            TrayStatus::NotConnected => "Not Connected",
35	            TrayStatus::Mounting => "Mounting...",
36	            TrayStatus::Syncing => "Syncing...",
37	            TrayStatus::Synced => "Synced",
38	            TrayStatus::Offline => "Offline",
39	            TrayStatus::Error(_) => "Error",
40	            TrayStatus::WriteParked => "Upload Failed",
41	        }
42	    }
43	
44	    /// Returns `true` when the app is authenticated and has (or had) a mounted filesystem.
45	    ///
46	    /// True for Syncing, Synced, Offline (connected but temporarily unreachable).
47	    /// False for NotConnected, Mounting, Error, WriteParked.
48	    pub fn is_connected(&self) -> bool {
49	        matches!(self, TrayStatus::Syncing | TrayStatus::Synced | TrayStatus::Offline)
50	    }
51	}
52	
53	#[cfg(test)]
54	mod tests {
55	    use super::*;
56	
57	    #[test]
58	    fn test_labels() {
59	        assert_eq!(TrayStatus::NotConnected.label(), "Not Connected");
60	        assert_eq!(TrayStatus::Mounting.label(), "Mounting...");
61	        assert_eq!(TrayStatus::Syncing.label(), "Syncing...");
62	        assert_eq!(TrayStatus::Synced.label(), "Synced");
63	        assert_eq!(TrayStatus::Offline.label(), "Offline");
64	        assert_eq!(TrayStatus::Error("disk full".into()).label(), "Error");
65	        assert_eq!(TrayStatus::WriteParked.label(), "Upload Failed");
66	    }
67	
68	    #[test]
69	    fn test_is_connected() {
70	        assert!(!TrayStatus::NotConnected.is_connected());
71	        assert!(!TrayStatus::Mounting.is_connected());
72	        assert!(TrayStatus::Syncing.is_connected());
73	        assert!(TrayStatus::Synced.is_connected());
74	        assert!(TrayStatus::Offline.is_connected());
75	        assert!(!TrayStatus::Error("oops".into()).is_connected());
76	        assert!(!TrayStatus::WriteParked.is_connected());
77	    }
78	}
79

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs",
  "content": "//! Sync daemon bridge for desktop app.\n//!\n//! Creates `cipherbox_sdk::SyncDaemon` with Tauri tray status callback.\n//! The sync daemon itself lives in the SDK crate -- this module provides\n//! the bridge that converts SDK `SyncStatus` to Tauri `TrayStatus`.\n\n// Re-export SDK types that the desktop app uses directly\npub use cipherbox_sdk::sync::SYNC_INTERVAL;\npub use cipherbox_sdk::SyncDaemon;\n\nuse std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};\nuse std::sync::Arc;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse tokio::sync::mpsc;\n\nuse cipherbox_sdk::SyncStatus;\n\nuse crate::tray::TrayStatus;\n\n/// Pure outcome of bridging one `SyncStatus` to the tray, computed without touching\n/// the OS — so the edge-trigger notification logic (D-10) is unit-testable.\n#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]\nstruct TrayBridgeOutcome {\n    /// Tray status to display (level-triggered steady-state indicator).\n    tray: TrayStatus,\n    /// New value for the \"last notified failed count\" watermark.\n    watermark: u32,\n    /// `Some(failed)` => fire a park notification for `failed` failures; `None` => stay silent.\n    notify_failed: Option<u32>,\n}\n\n/// Decide the tray status, notification watermark, and whether to fire an OS toast for a\n/// given `SyncStatus`, given the last failed count we already notified on (`prev_watermark`).\n///\n/// Edge-trigger rule (D-10): the park toast fires only when the parked-failure count\n/// *rises* above the last value we notified on, so a steady stream of poll cycles at the\n/// same failure level notifies once, not every ~30s poll. The watermark resets to 0 when\n/// the journal clears (Idle, or `WriteParked` with `failed == 0`) so a later failure\n/// notifies again. It is intentionally preserved (not reset) on Syncing — the daemon emits\n/// Syncing at the start of every cycle, and resetting there would re-arm the toast each\n/// poll and reintroduce the spam.\nfn bridge_status(status: &SyncStatus, prev_watermark: u32) -> TrayBridgeOutcome {\n    match status {\n        SyncStatus::Idle => TrayBridgeOutcome {\n            tray: TrayStatus::Synced,\n            watermark: 0,\n            notify_failed: None,\n        },\n        SyncStatus::Syncing => TrayBridgeOutcome {\n            tray: TrayStatus::Syncing,\n            watermark: prev_watermark, // preserved on purpose — see doc comment\n            notify_failed: None,\n        },\n        SyncStatus::Error(e) if e == \"Offline\" => TrayBridgeOutcome {\n            tray: TrayStatus::Offline,\n            watermark: prev_watermark,\n            notify_failed: None,\n        },\n        SyncStatus::Error(e) => TrayBridgeOutcome {\n            tray: TrayStatus::Error(e.clone()),\n            watermark: prev_watermark,\n            notify_failed: None,\n        },\n        SyncStatus::WriteParked { failed, .. } if *failed > 0 => TrayBridgeOutcome {\n            tray: TrayStatus::WriteParked,\n            watermark: *failed,\n            notify_failed: if *failed > prev_watermark {\n                Some(*failed)\n            } else {\n                None\n            },\n        },\n        // failed == 0: pending-only state — transient retries are silent (D-10).\n        SyncStatus::WriteParked { .. } => TrayBridgeOutcome {\n            tray: TrayStatus::Syncing,\n            watermark: 0,\n            notify_failed: None,\n        },\n    }\n}\n\n/// Start the SDK sync daemon with a Tauri tray status bridge.\n///\n/// The `sync_now_rx` channel receives manual sync triggers from the tray menu.\n/// The `write_queue` must point at the same cb-journal directory the FUSE layer writes\n/// so the daemon can observe `Failed` entries and surface them via `WriteParked` (CR-07).\n/// Returns a `SyncDaemon` that should be run in a spawned tokio task.\npub fn create_sync_daemon(\n    state: Arc<cipherbox_sdk::KeyState>,\n    app_handle: tauri::AppHandle,\n    poll_interval: Duration,\n    sync_now_rx: mpsc::Receiver<()>,\n    write_queue: cipherbox_sdk::WriteQueue,\n) -> SyncDaemon {\n    let app = app_handle;\n    // Watermark of the last parked-failure count we fired an OS toast for (D-10). The\n    // pure decision lives in `bridge_status`; this callback applies it and performs the\n    // OS side effects (notification + tray update).\n    let last_notified_failed = Arc::new(AtomicU32::new(0));\n    let status_callback = Arc::new(move |status: SyncStatus| {\n        // The daemon invokes this serially per poll cycle, so load+store is equivalent\n        // to a swap of the watermark.\n        let prev = last_notified_failed.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let outcome = bridge_status(&status, prev);\n        last_notified_failed.store(outcome.watermark, Ordering::Relaxed);\n\n        if let Some(failed) = outcome.notify_failed {\n            // ZK-safe neutral copy — no file names or paths that could leak vault\n            // contents into OS notification logs.\n            let msg = format!(\"{} pending upload(s) failed and require attention.\", failed);\n            if let Err(e) = crate::tray::send_write_parked_notification(&app, &msg) {\n                log::warn!(\"Failed to send write-parked notification: {}\", e);\n            }\n        }\n        let _ = crate::tray::update_tray_status(&app, &outcome.tray);\n    });\n\n    SyncDaemon::new(state, status_callback, poll_interval, sync_now_rx, write_queue)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    fn parked(pending: u32, failed: u32) -> SyncStatus {\n        SyncStatus::WriteParked { pending, failed }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn idle_marks_synced_and_clears_watermark() {\n        let out = bridge_status(&SyncStatus::Idle, 3);\n        assert_eq!(out.tray, TrayStatus::Synced);\n        assert_eq!(out.watermark, 0);\n        assert_eq!(out.notify_failed, None);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn syncing_preserves_watermark_and_is_silent() {\n        // Critical: Syncing must NOT reset the watermark, or the daemon's per-cycle\n        // Syncing emit would re-arm the toast every poll (D-10 spam regression).\n        let out = bridge_status(&SyncStatus::Syncing, 2);\n        assert_eq!(out.tray, TrayStatus::Syncing);\n        assert_eq!(out.watermark, 2);\n        assert_eq!(out.notify_failed, None);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn offline_and_error_preserve_watermark_silently() {\n        let off = bridge_status(&SyncStatus::Error(\"Offline\".to_string()), 5);\n        assert_eq!(off.tray, TrayStatus::Offline);\n        assert_eq!(off.watermark, 5);\n        assert_eq!(off.notify_failed, None);\n\n        let err = bridge_status(&SyncStatus::Error(\"boom\".to_string()), 5);\n        assert_eq!(err.tray, TrayStatus::Error(\"boom\".to_string()));\n        assert_eq!(err.watermark, 5);\n        assert_eq!(err.notify_failed, None);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn first_parked_failure_notifies() {\n        let out = bridge_status(&parked(0, 2), 0);\n        assert_eq!(out.tray, TrayStatus::WriteParked);\n        assert_eq!(out.watermark, 2);\n        assert_eq!(out.notify_failed, Some(2));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn same_failure_level_does_not_renotify() {\n        let out = bridge_status(&parked(0, 2), 2);\n        assert_eq!(out.tray, TrayStatus::WriteParked);\n        assert_eq!(out.watermark, 2);\n        assert_eq!(out.notify_failed, None);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn rising_failure_count_renotifies() {\n        let out = bridge_status(&parked(1, 3), 2);\n        assert_eq!(out.tray, TrayStatus::WriteParked);\n        assert_eq!(out.watermark, 3);\n        assert_eq!(out.notify_failed, Some(3));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn pending_only_is_silent_and_clears_watermark() {\n        let out = bridge_status(&parked(4, 0), 2);\n        assert_eq!(out.tray, TrayStatus::Syncing);\n        assert_eq!(out.watermark, 0);\n        assert_eq!(out.notify_failed, None);\n    }\n\n    /// End-to-end edge-trigger trace: notify once on a new failure, stay silent while it\n    /// persists (including across the per-poll Syncing emit), re-notify only when it\n    /// rises, and re-arm after the journal clears.\n    #[test]\n    fn edge_trigger_sequence_notifies_once_per_new_failure() {\n        let mut wm = 0u32;\n        let mut step = |s: SyncStatus| {\n            let o = bridge_status(&s, wm);\n            wm = o.watermark;\n            o.notify_failed\n        };\n        assert_eq!(step(parked(0, 1)), Some(1)); // new failure -> notify\n        assert_eq!(step(SyncStatus::Syncing), None); // poll cycle -> silent, watermark kept\n        assert_eq!(step(parked(0, 1)), None); // same level -> silent\n        assert_eq!(step(parked(0, 2)), Some(2)); // rose -> notify\n        assert_eq!(step(SyncStatus::Idle), None); // journal cleared -> reset\n        assert_eq!(step(parked(0, 1)), Some(1)); // re-armed -> notify again\n    }\n}\n"
}

Both parts landed and pushed to PR #487:
- `4d79cfc6d refactor(desktop): extract and unit-test sync tray-bridge edge-trigger`
- `5adcfb86d ci: surface Rust crate coverage in codecov via a rust flag`

Branch synced; CI will re-run and the codecov comment will refresh.

## What changed

**Part 1 — `sync/mod.rs`** (verified: 8/8 tests pass, behavior preserved)
Extracted the `SyncStatus`→`TrayStatus` mapping + the D-10 edge-trigger decision into a pure `bridge_status()`; the daemon closure now just applies it + does the OS side effects. The 8 tests lock down the anti-spam logic that had zero coverage — notably that **Syncing preserves the watermark** (resetting it would re-arm the toast every poll, the exact bug `5b6455c34` fixed).

**Part 2 — codecov** (`codecov.yml` + `ci.yml`, YAML validated)
Added a `rust` flag (`crates/**`) and a second upload of the existing `desktop-lcov.info` under it. The workspace `llvm-cov` already collected all crate coverage — it was just being dropped by the `desktop` flag's path filter. Now the 40 fuse + 43 sdk tests **count**.

## What to expect on the next codecov run

- A new **`rust` flag** appears showing real `crates/**` coverage — `queue.rs`/helpers land high (well-tested); `lib.rs` replay lands low (network-bound, captured in the deferred todos).
- Honest tradeoff: surfacing the crates also makes the **untested replay code visible in patch %**, so the headline patch number may not jump — but it now reflects reality instead of grading only Tauri glue. Both the `rust` status and the `patch` check are `informational: true`, so neither blocks merge.

Net: the desktop edge-trigger logic is now tested, and your Rust durability code is finally measured instead of silently discarded. The remaining low spots (FUSE replay, mount/tray glue) are the genuinely-untestable bits already covered by the 3-platform UAT and tracked as todos.

========== THREAD #1 (apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/tray/status.rs:50) ==========
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**`WriteParked` should remain a connected state for tray actions.**

Line 47 currently treats `WriteParked` as disconnected. That makes the tray effectively non-actionable in parked-failure state (e.g., logout/open/sync paths become disabled via menu gating), even though the user is still authenticated and needs recovery actions.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-    /// False for NotConnected, Mounting, Error, WriteParked.
+    /// False for NotConnected, Mounting, Error.
     pub fn is_connected(&self) -> bool {
-        matches!(self, TrayStatus::Syncing | TrayStatus::Synced | TrayStatus::Offline)
+        matches!(
+            self,
+            TrayStatus::Syncing | TrayStatus::Synced | TrayStatus::Offline | TrayStatus::WriteParked
+        )
     }
```

And update menu gating in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/tray/mod.rs` so `WriteParked` still allows recovery actions (open/sync/logout).
</details>

</details>

**Refactor `resolve_sequence` to return typed `ApiError` instead of converting to `String`.**

The check at line 1396 uses `e.to_lowercase().contains("not found")` to detect first-publish cases, but this relies on fragile string matching. A typed `ApiError::IpnsNotFound` variant already exists in the API client (crates/api-client/src/error.rs), but `resolve_sequence` converts all errors to `String` (line 245: `Err(format!(...))`), forcing callers to use string matching.

Refactor `resolve_sequence` to return `Result<u64, ApiError>` instead of `Result<u64, String>`. This allows pattern matching on `ApiError::IpnsNotFound` at line 1396 instead of fragile substring checks, making the code more robust and maintainable.

function currently converts all errors to `String` format, forcing the caller to
use fragile string matching with `to_lowercase().contains("not found")`.
Refactor the `resolve_sequence` function to return `Result<u64, ApiError>`
instead of `Result<u64, String>`, ensuring errors are returned as the typed
`ApiError` enum rather than being converted to strings. Update the error
handling at the call site in the match statement to use pattern matching on
`ApiError::IpnsNotFound` instead of substring checking, replacing the string
matching logic with direct pattern matching against the specific error variant.

**Thread #1 (valid, minimal fix):** `is_connected()` gates only the **Logout** menu item (line 96). Open is gated by `is_mounted`, Sync by `is_syncable` — so CodeRabbit's open/sync claim is slightly off, but the core point stands: in `WriteParked` the user is authenticated yet **can't log out**. That's a real (small) bug. I'll apply the suggested `is_connected` change (enables Logout) + fix the test; I'll leave Open/Sync gating alone (separate, intentional).

**Thread #2 (defer — it's the tracked todo):** `resolve_sequence` has **9 call sites**; returning typed `ApiError` touches all of them + error plumbing. **Thread #1 — fixed.** Added `TrayStatus::WriteParked` to `is_connected()` so the **Logout** item (the only thing it gates, `tray/mod.rs:96`) stays enabled for an authenticated user with parked uploads; updated the unit test (2/2 pass). I corrected CodeRabbit's claim in the reply: Open/Sync are gated by `is_mounted`/`is_syncable`, not `is_connected`, so they were intentionally left alone — only Logout was actually affected.

**Thread #2 — deferred with tracking.** This is the typed-error refactor I'd already captured as a todo this session. Net: both CodeRabbit threads addressed — one fixed, one resolved with a clear tracked-follow-up rationale.

> <details>

> <details>
> <summary>apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs (1)</summary><blockquote>
> 
> `28-36`: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_
> 
> **Prevent duplicate sync-daemon spawns (idempotency gap).**
> 
> Line 28 spawns unconditionally, and Lines 33-36 replace `sync_trigger` without shutting down or reusing an existing daemon. Re-entering auth/setup or calling the IPC command again can leave multiple daemon loops running concurrently (duplicate polling/status updates/notifications).
> 
> <details>
> <summary>Suggested fix</summary>
> 
> ```diff
>  pub fn spawn_sync_daemon(app: tauri::AppHandle, state: &AppState) -> Result<(), String> {
> +    // Idempotent start: if already started, do nothing.
> +    if let Ok(guard) = state.sync_trigger.read() {
> +        if guard.is_some() {
> +            log::debug!("Sync daemon already running; skipping spawn");
> +            return Ok(());
> +        }
> +    }
> +
>      log::info!("Starting background sync daemon");
> ```
> </details>
> 
>    
> 
> 
> Also applies to: 51-60
> 
> <details>
> <summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>
> 
> ```
> Verify each finding against current code. Fix only still-valid issues, skip the
> rest with a brief reason, keep changes minimal, and validate.
> 
> In `@apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs` around lines 28 - 36, The
> spawn_sync_daemon function unconditionally spawns a new daemon without checking
> if one is already running, which can result in multiple concurrent daemon
> instances. Before creating a new channel and spawning the daemon, check if
> state.sync_trigger already contains an existing sender. If it does, either
> return early since a daemon is already active, or first shut down the existing
> daemon by sending a termination signal before creating and storing the new
> sender. This ensures idempotency and prevents duplicate polling and status
> updates.
> ```
> 
> </details>
>

<summary>🧹 Nitpick comments (2)</summary><blockquote>

<details>
<summary>crates/fuse/src/lib.rs (1)</summary><blockquote>

`1038-1058`: _💤 Low value_

**Minor: `depth` variable counts nodes visited, not tree depth.**

The variable `depth` is incremented per node popped from the queue (line 1058), so it actually counts total nodes visited rather than tree depth. For BFS, this means a wide tree with many folders at level 1 could exhaust the cap before reaching a deeper nested folder.

The cap still effectively bounds network round trips (32 resolve+fetch operations), so it's functionally correct for its purpose. The comment at line 1039 ("limits network round trips") is accurate, but the variable name `depth` is slightly misleading.

This is minor and doesn't affect correctness for typical folder hierarchies.

many folders at the same level could hit the cap before reaching deeper nested
folders. Rename the `depth` variable to a more accurate name like
`nodes_visited` or `operations_count` throughout the function to better reflect
that it's tracking the number of BFS operations/nodes processed rather than tree
depth. Update the variable name in its initialization near the queue setup, the
comparison in the if statement checking MAX_RESOLVE_DEPTH, and the increment
operation.
```

**Test `journal_no_plaintext` doesn't actually test the probe string.**

The `plaintext_probe` variable is defined at line 437 but never inserted into the test entry. The assertion at lines 441-444 will always pass trivially since the probe was never part of the data. The test still validates that certain forbidden keys (`"plaintext"`, `"parent_ino"`) don't appear, which is useful, but the probe-based assertion is effectively a no-op.

Consider either removing the unused probe or constructing a test entry that would contain plaintext if the schema were wrong.

1	//! Background sync daemon command.
2	
3	use tauri::State;
4	
5	use crate::state::AppState;
6	
7	/// Start the background sync daemon.
8	///
9	/// Exposed as an IPC command for completeness, but the daemon is normally started
10	/// automatically from the post-mount auth path (`complete_auth_setup`) so every
11	/// login flow gets a running daemon. Delegates to [`spawn_sync_daemon`].
12	#[tauri::command]
13	pub async fn start_sync_daemon(
14	    app: tauri::AppHandle,
15	    state: State<'_, AppState>,
16	) -> Result<(), String> {
17	    spawn_sync_daemon(app, state.inner())
18	}
19	
20	/// Create the sync channel, store its sender in AppState for the tray menu, and
21	/// spawn the background sync daemon.
22	///
23	/// Constructs a `WriteQueue` pointing at `<data_local_dir>/cipherbox/cb-journal` —
24	/// the same path the FUSE mount uses — so the daemon observes the same on-disk
25	/// entries the FUSE layer writes (CR-07). The daemon is what surfaces parked
26	/// writes via `WriteParked` notifications, so it MUST be started once the vault
27	/// is mounted; otherwise failed uploads never reach the user (G-43-UAT-01).
28	pub fn spawn_sync_daemon(app: tauri::AppHandle, state: &AppState) -> Result<(), String> {
29	    log::info!("Starting background sync daemon");
30	
31	    let (tx, rx) = tokio::sync::mpsc::channel::<()>(1);
32	
33	    // Store the sender in AppState so the tray "Sync Now" button can trigger syncs
34	    if let Ok(mut guard) = state.sync_trigger.write() {
35	        *guard = Some(tx);
36	    }
37	
38	    // Build the journal directory using the same resolution as the FUSE mount
39	    // (apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs) so daemon and FUSE share the
40	    // same on-disk journal (CR-07).
41	    let journal_dir = dirs::data_local_dir()
42	        .unwrap_or_else(std::env::temp_dir)
43	        .join("cipherbox")
44	        .join("cb-journal");
45	    std::fs::create_dir_all(&journal_dir)
46	        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to create journal directory: {}", e))?;
47	    let write_queue = cipherbox_sdk::WriteQueue::new(journal_dir, 5);
48	
49	    let sdk_state = state.sdk.clone();
50	
51	    tokio::spawn(async move {
52	        let mut daemon = crate::sync::create_sync_daemon(
53	            sdk_state,
54	            app,
55	            crate::sync::SYNC_INTERVAL,
56	            rx,
57	            write_queue,
58	        );
59	        daemon.run().await;
60	    });
61	
62	    log::info!("Sync daemon spawned");
63	    Ok(())
64	}
65	
```

1038	    // Depth cap: limits network round trips and prevents infinite loops (WR-02).

> AGENT

All three triaged with the code in hand:

- **#1 (Major, outside-diff):** Real bug. `spawn_sync_daemon` overwrites `sync_trigger` and `tokio::spawn`s unconditionally — calling it twice (re-auth / IPC) leaves **two daemon loops polling**, doubling every status update *and* `WriteParked` notification. That directly undermines this phase's D-10 anti-spam work, so it's in-scope to fix. → add idempotency guard.
- **#2 (nitpick):** Valid — `depth` counts nodes visited, not depth. → rename `depth`/`MAX_RESOLVE_DEPTH` for accuracy.
- **#3 (nitpick):** Valid — `plaintext_probe` is never inserted, so that assertion is a no-op. → fix the test.

Let me check the test helper to decide whether to make #3 meaningful or remove the no-op:

  "new_string": "pub fn spawn_sync_daemon(app: tauri::AppHandle, state: &AppState) -> Result<(), String> {\n    // Idempotent: if a daemon is already running, keep it. The daemon reads auth/vault\n    // state from the shared KeyState each poll, so it adapts across re-auth without a\n    // restart — spawning a second loop would leave two daemons polling and double every\n    // status update and WriteParked notification (the D-10 spam this phase prevents).\n    if let Ok(guard) = state.sync_trigger.read() {\n        if guard.is_some() {\n            log::debug!(\"Sync daemon already running; skipping spawn\");\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n    }\n\n    log::info!(\"Starting background sync daemon\");\n\n    let (tx, rx) = tokio::sync::mpsc::channel::<()>(1);"
}

> AGENT

All three handled and pushed. Here's the wrap-up.

## Review 4492966857 — all 3 addressed

| Comment | Severity | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Duplicate sync-daemon spawns (outside-diff) | 🟠 Major | **Fixed** `29be9d1da` — idempotency guard in `spawn_sync_daemon` |
| `depth` misnamed in `resolve_folder_key` | nitpick | **Fixed** `c65b79779` — renamed to `nodes_visited`/`MAX_RESOLVE_NODES` |
| `journal_no_plaintext` no-op probe | nitpick | **Fixed** `c65b79779` — now asserts raw key bytes don't leak (meaningful) |

The Major one was the worth-having catch: calling `spawn_sync_daemon` twice (re-auth or the IPC command) left two daemon loops polling, which would have **doubled the `WriteParked` notifications** — directly undermining this phase's D-10 anti-spam work. The guard returns early if a daemon's already running; since it reads auth/vault state from the shared `KeyState` each poll, one long-lived daemon adapts across re-auth without a restart.

**Verified:** `cargo test -p cipherbox-fuse -p cipherbox-sdk` → 40 + 43 pass (incl. the strengthened journal test); `cargo check -p cipherbox-desktop` clean.

**Mechanics:** these were CodeRabbit *body* comments (out-of-diff/nitpick), not inline threads, so there's nothing to "resolve" via the thread API — I posted a disposition summary on the PR ([comment](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/487#issuecomment-4701998946)) and CodeRabbit will re-review on the push.

Combined with the 2 inline threads resolved earlier, **all CodeRabbit feedback on PR #487 is now handled.** Branch synced at `c65b79779`; CI will re-run.

1	---
2	created: 2026-06-14T12:37:25.820Z
3	title: Consolidate fuser and winfsp journal write paths
4	area: desktop-fuse
5	files:
6	  - crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs
7	  - crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs
8	  - crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs
9	  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs
10	---
11	
12	## Problem
13	
14	The fuser release path (`crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs`) and the winfsp cleanup path
15	(`crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs`) carry ~150 lines of near-identical
16	code: the local `UploadSpawnParams` struct, the prepare closure that builds the
17	`UploadFile`/`MkdirPublish` journal entry, the `journal.put` + deferred-mutation
18	sequence, and the spawn-upload closure. The mkdir journal-entry construction is
19	similarly duplicated between `write_ops.rs` and the winfsp `write_ops.rs`.
20	
21	The two copies have already begun to drift (CR-04/CR-05/CR-07 fixes had to be applied
22	twice; the fuser struct briefly carried a dead `journal_entry_id` field the winfsp one
23	did not). Every future change to journal-entry shape, the D-04 fsync-before-ack
24	ordering, or the D-05 plaintext handling must be made twice and kept in sync by hand.
25	
26	Flagged as the #1 item by all four agents in the phase-43 `/simplify` review and
27	deferred from the cleanup pass (commit a1ec69f1b) as too large/risky for that pass.
28	
29	## Solution
30	
31	Lift the platform-independent core into shared `CipherBoxFS` methods in
32	`crates/fuse/src/lib.rs` (or a `write_common` module), e.g.
33	`build_upload_journal_entry(...) -> Result<(JournalEntry, UploadSpawnParams), String>`,
34	`build_mkdir_journal_entry(...) -> JournalEntry`, and a `spawn_upload(params)`. The
35	platform layers keep only what genuinely differs: the reply/cleanup ordering
36	(`reply.ok()` / `reply.error(EIO)` on fuser vs. WinFsp's void `Cleanup`).
37	
38	Constraints: this is the most security-sensitive path — the D-04 fsync-before-ack and
39	D-05 plaintext-zeroize ordering MUST be preserved exactly, and the deferred-mutation
40	ordering (CR-04) must stay after `journal.put`. The winfsp half is not locally
41	compilable on macOS, so verify via the winfsp CI gate (`cargo check --features winfsp`)
42	and the desktop E2E. Pairs naturally with the Option<String> key helper and the
43	created_at/now_ms helpers.
44

## `2026-06-14-improve-phase-43-rust-test-coverage.md`

I pulled the real codecov breakdown first so it's actionable — 638 missing lines, but the patch status is `informational` (non-blocking), so this is genuinely a longer-term quality item, not a merge blocker. Sorted into three tiers by effort/value:

**Tier 1 — quick wins, no harness (~48 lines).** The standout: `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs` has four **pure functions with zero tests** — `sanitize_error`, `regex_replace_paths`, `regex_replace_tokens`, `is_network_error`. Those are log-redaction helpers (they scrub filesystem paths and tokens out of error strings), so testing them has real security value beyond the number. Plus the residual branches in `queue.rs` (97.5%) and `sync/mod.rs` (87.4%).

**Tier 2 — the bulk (~522 lines), needs trait seams.** `lib.rs` replay orchestration + the `read_ops.rs`/`write_ops.rs` FUSE callbacks only run against a mounted FS + live coordinator. Unit-testing them means injecting the SDK client / coordinator / journal behind traits — which is **directly enabled by** the two write-path consolidation todos already captured, so I cross-linked them and noted Tier 2 should land *after* those.

**Tier 3 — accept the gap (~65 lines).** The Tauri `#[tauri::command]` glue is already exercised by the headless UAT harness; codecov just doesn't see that run. Flagged as a known accepted gap rather than something to chase with contrived AppHandle unit tests.

That's the whole story on PR #487's coverage now — the comment was the last open thread, and this closes it out as a tracked follow-up rather than scope-creeping into the phase.
