_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Make daemon idempotency check-and-set atomic**

Current logic has a race: two callers can pass the read check and both spawn. Also, poisoned lock errors are ignored. Use one write-lock section to check and store the sender atomically, and return an error if lock acquisition fails.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
-    if let Ok(guard) = state.sync_trigger.read() {
-        if guard.is_some() {
-            log::debug!("Sync daemon already running; skipping spawn");
-            return Ok(());
-        }
-    }
-
     log::info!("Starting background sync daemon");
 
     let (tx, rx) = tokio::sync::mpsc::channel::<()>(1);
 
-    // Store the sender in AppState so the tray "Sync Now" button can trigger syncs
-    if let Ok(mut guard) = state.sync_trigger.write() {
-        *guard = Some(tx);
-    }
+    // Atomically enforce singleton daemon registration.
+    {
+        let mut guard = state
+            .sync_trigger
+            .write()
+            .map_err(|_| "sync_trigger lock poisoned".to_string())?;
+        if guard.is_some() {
+            log::debug!("Sync daemon already running; skipping spawn");
+            return Ok(());
+        }
+        *guard = Some(tx);
+    }
```
</details>







Also applies to: 45-47

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```
Verify each finding against current code. Fix only still-valid issues, skip the
rest with a brief reason, keep changes minimal, and validate.

In `@apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs` around lines 33 - 38, The
current implementation has a race condition where the check for whether the sync
daemon is already running uses a read lock, allowing multiple callers to pass
the check before any can set the value, potentially spawning multiple daemons.
Additionally, poisoned lock errors are being silently ignored. Replace the read
lock check with a single write lock section that atomically checks if the sync
daemon sender exists and sets it in one operation; if the write lock acquisition
fails (including poisoned lock cases), return an error rather than ignoring it.
This ensures only one daemon is spawned across concurrent calls to the sync
command function.
```

```
20	/// Create the sync channel, store its sender in AppState for the tray menu, and
21	/// spawn the background sync daemon.
22	///
23	/// Constructs a `WriteQueue` pointing at `<data_local_dir>/cipherbox/cb-journal` —
24	/// the same path the FUSE mount uses — so the daemon observes the same on-disk
25	/// entries the FUSE layer writes (CR-07). The daemon is what surfaces parked
26	/// writes via `WriteParked` notifications, so it MUST be started once the vault
27	/// is mounted; otherwise failed uploads never reach the user (G-43-UAT-01).
28	pub fn spawn_sync_daemon(app: tauri::AppHandle, state: &AppState) -> Result<(), String> {
29	    // Idempotent: if a daemon is already running, keep it. The daemon reads auth/vault
30	    // state from the shared KeyState each poll, so it adapts across re-auth without a
31	    // restart — spawning a second loop would leave two daemons polling and double every
32	    // status update and WriteParked notification (the D-10 spam this phase prevents).
33	    if let Ok(guard) = state.sync_trigger.read() {
34	        if guard.is_some() {
35	            log::debug!("Sync daemon already running; skipping spawn");
36	            return Ok(());
37	        }
38	    }
39	
40	    log::info!("Starting background sync daemon");
41	
42	    let (tx, rx) = tokio::sync::mpsc::channel::<()>(1);
43	
44	    // Store the sender in AppState so the tray "Sync Now" button can trigger syncs
45	    if let Ok(mut guard) = state.sync_trigger.write() {
46	        *guard = Some(tx);
47	    }
48	
49	    // Build the journal directory using the same resolution as the FUSE mount
50	    // (apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs) so daemon and FUSE share the
51	    // same on-disk journal (CR-07).
52	    let journal_dir = dirs::data_local_dir()
53	        .unwrap_or_else(std::env::temp_dir)
54	        .join("cipherbox")
55	        .join("cb-journal");
56	    std::fs::create_dir_all(&journal_dir)
57	        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to create journal directory: {}", e))?;
58	    let write_queue = cipherbox_sdk::WriteQueue::new(journal_dir, 5);
59	
60	    let sdk_state = state.sdk.clone();
61	
62	    tokio::spawn(async move {
63	        let mut daemon = crate::sync::create_sync_daemon(
64	            sdk_state,
65	            app,
66	            crate::sync::SYNC_INTERVAL,
67	            rx,
68	            write_queue,
69	        );
70	        daemon.run().await;
71	    });
72	
73	    log::info!("Sync daemon spawned");
74	    Ok(())
75	}
76	
```

The daemon reads auth/vault\n    // state from the shared KeyState each poll, so it adapts across re-auth without a\n    // restart — spawning a second loop would leave two daemons polling and double every\n    // status update and WriteParked notification (the D-10 spam this phase prevents).\n    if let Ok(guard) = state.sync_trigger.read() {\n        if guard.is_some() {\n            log::debug!(\"Sync daemon already running; skipping spawn\");\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n    }\n\n    log::info!(\"Starting background sync daemon\");\n\n    let (tx, rx) = tokio::sync::mpsc::channel::<()>(1);\n\n    // Store the sender in AppState so the tray \"Sync Now\" button can trigger syncs\n    if let Ok(mut guard) = state.sync_trigger.write() {\n        *guard = Some(tx);\n    }",

The daemon reads\n    // auth/vault state from the shared KeyState each poll, so it adapts across re-auth\n    // without a restart — spawning a second loop would leave two daemons polling and\n    // double every status update and WriteParked notification (the D-10 spam this phase\n    // prevents).\n    let (tx, rx) = tokio::sync::mpsc::channel::<()>(1);\n\n    // Check-and-set the trigger sender under a single write lock so two concurrent\n    // callers can't both pass the \"already running?\" check and each spawn a daemon. A\n    // poisoned lock is surfaced as an error rather than silently ignored — ignoring it\n    // would spawn a daemon whose sender the tray \"Sync Now\" button could never reach.\n    {\n        let mut guard = state\n            .sync_trigger\n            .write()\n            .map_err(|_| \"sync_trigger lock poisoned\".to_string())?;\n        if guard.is_some() {\n            log::debug!(\"Sync daemon already running; skipping spawn\");\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n        *guard = Some(tx);\n    }\n\n    log::info!(\"Starting background sync daemon\");"

## PR #487 — review threads resolved (2/2)

| Thread | File:line | Disposition | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| Daemon idempotency race | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs:38` | ✅ **Fixed** | Commit `7fe5ac5e1` |
| Pin codecov action to SHA | `.github/workflows/ci.yml:671` | ⏭️ **Not applicable (scope)** | Replied + resolved |

### Fixed — the real bug

CodeRabbit caught a genuine TOCTOU race in the idempotency guard I'd just added in `29be9d1da`: it checked `sync_trigger` under a `read()` lock, then stored the sender under a *separate* `write()` lock. Two concurrent callers could both pass the read check and each spawn a daemon — defeating the very guard's purpose and doubling the `WriteParked` notifications this phase works to dedupe (D-10). It also silently swallowed a poisoned lock, which would spawn a daemon whose sender the tray "Sync Now" button could never reach.

Fix collapses check-and-set into one write-lock critical section and propagates poisoning as `Err`. `cargo check -p cipherbox-desktop --features fuse` passes. (No fuse/sdk source touched, so those suites didn't need re-running; the change is Rust-only, so the skill's `pnpm typecheck`/`test` steps don't apply.)

### Declined — and why it's the right call

The SHA-pin suggestion is sound supply-chain advice in the abstract, but I checked the repo first: **111 `uses:` references, zero SHA pins** — every action is a version tag. Line 671 deliberately matches the two pre-existing `codecov/codecov-action@v7` refs (335, 660) it was copied from, so my change introduced no new inconsistency. Pinning only codecov would make it the lone SHA-pinned action and break the convention. A repo-wide migration to immutable SHAs (plus the `excessive-permissions` blocks zizmor also flagged) is a separate CI-hardening effort outside this phase-43 PR — I said as much in the thread reply and resolved it rather than scope-creeping onto one of three identical refs.

Branch is at `7fe5ac5e1`, pushed; CI will re-run.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-14-pin-github-actions-to-immutable-shas.md",
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-14T14:40:38.000Z\ntitle: Pin GitHub Actions to immutable SHAs (CI supply-chain hardening)\narea: ci\nfiles:\n  - .github/workflows/ci.yml\n  - .github/workflows/ci-e2e.yml\n  - .github/workflows/codecov-base.yml\n  - .github/workflows/deploy-landing.yml\n  - .github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml\n  - .github/workflows/desktop-e2e.yml\n  - .github/workflows/desktop-staging-release.yml\n  - .github/workflows/load-test.yml\n  - .github/workflows/pr-release-preview.yml\n  - .github/workflows/pr-title.yml\n  - .github/workflows/release-gate.yml\n  - .github/workflows/release-please.yml\n  - .github/workflows/tag-staging.yml\n  - .github/workflows/web-e2e.yml\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nEvery third-party GitHub Action across the 14 workflow files is referenced by a\n**mutable version tag** — 111 `uses:` refs, **zero** pinned to a commit SHA. A tag\nlike `@v6` can be force-moved by the action's maintainer (or an attacker who\ncompromises their account) to point at malicious code, which would then run in CI with\naccess to repo secrets. GitHub's own hardening guidance and the `zizmor` SAST linter\nboth flag this (`unpinned-uses`, \"required by blanket policy\").\n\nSurfaced by CodeRabbit + zizmor on PR #487 (the three `codecov/codecov-action@v7`\nrefs at `ci.yml` lines 335/660/671). Deliberately **not** fixed in that PR: pinning\nonly codecov would have made it the lone SHA-pinned action among 111 and broken the\nrepo-wide convention. This todo tracks doing it consistently across the whole suite.\n\nDistinct third-party actions in use (count) — these are what need pinning:\n\n```\n33  actions/checkout@v6              5  actions/upload-artifact@v7\n20  actions/setup-node@v6           4  codecov/codecov-action@v7\n18  pnpm/action-setup@v6            3  appleboy/ssh-action@v1.2.5\n 9  actions/cache@v5                3  appleboy/scp-action@v1.0.0\n 6  tauri-apps/tauri-action@v0      2  dorny/paths-filter@v4\n 2  docker/login-action@v4          2  docker/build-push-action@v7\n 1  ikalnytskyi/action-setup-postgres@v8   1  googleapis/release-please-action@v5\n 1  actions/download-artifact@v8    1  actions/create-github-app-token@v3\n```\n\nThe higher-risk ones are the non-GitHub-owned, lower-profile publishers\n(`appleboy/*`, `dorny/*`, `ikalnytskyi/*`, `tauri-apps/*`) — prioritize those if doing\nit incrementally. The three `./.github/workflows/*.yml` reusable-workflow refs are\ninternal and do NOT need SHA pinning.\n\n## Solution\n\nPin every third-party action to a full 40-char commit SHA with a trailing version\ncomment, e.g.:\n\n```yaml\n- uses: actions/checkout@<40-char-sha> # v6.0.0\n```\n\n**Critical companion step — otherwise this regresses into staleness:** enable\nDependabot for the `github-actions` ecosystem (`.github/dependabot.yml`) so it bumps\nthe pinned SHAs (and updates the version comments) on new releases. Without it, pinning\nfreezes CI on whatever SHA was current and silently stops receiving security patches —\nthe exact maintenance-drift trade-off called out when this was deferred on PR #487.\n\nRecommended approach:\n\n1. Use a tool to do the bulk conversion rather than hand-editing 111 refs —\n   [`pinact`](https://github.com/suzuki-shunsuke/pinact) or\n   [`ratchet`](https://github.com/sethvargo/ratchet) both resolve tags→SHAs and add\n   version comments idempotently.\n2. Add/extend `.github/dependabot.yml` with a `package-ecosystem: \"github-actions\"`\n   entry so pins stay current.\n3. (Optional, prevents regressions) Add a `zizmor` CI gate so any newly introduced\n   unpinned ref fails the build. This also covers the related zizmor finding from the\n   same scan: the `cargo-linux` job in `ci.yml` (and any others) lacking a job-level\n   `permissions:` block (`excessive-permissions`) — add least-privilege `permissions:`\n   while in here.\n\nScope note: pure CI hardening, no app-code or runtime impact. Verify by re-running the\nfull workflow suite on a PR after conversion (the SHAs must resolve to the same action\nversions currently in use, so behavior should be unchanged).\n"
}
